A10-P POS-A ANDROID COMMAND SPECIFICATION FOR CODEX for A10-P SYM VERSION

Source document:
- A10-P Programmer's Manual, Personal ID Number Pad with EMV Level 2 Transaction Capabilities, POS-A Specification, PM 0122-C3, Revision 6, 2023-02-17.
- This TXT is a Codex-oriented extraction and implementation guide covering the message format section, all command/message sections, and supporting appendices.

Implementation target:
- Build an Android application/library that can exchange A10-P POS-A messages with the device.
- The Android implementation should be byte-accurate. Treat the payload text as ASCII/hex strings exactly where the command says so.
- Build a reusable transport layer, frame encoder/decoder, command model classes, response model classes, and flow orchestration for multi-step EMV/MSR/MIFARE/JPEG/display/key-entry operations.

Critical protocol rules:
1. Frame types:
   - Transaction/application frames generally use: <STX>[message ID][data]<ETX>[LRC]
   - Administration frames generally use: <SI>[message ID][data]<SO>[LRC]
2. Control bytes:
   - STX = 0x02
   - ETX = 0x03
   - ACK = 0x06
   - SI  = 0x0F
   - SO  = 0x0E
   - NAK = 0x15
   - SUB = 0x1A
   - FS  = 0x1C
   - GS  = 0x1D
   - DC1 = 0x11
   - DC2 = 0x12
   - EOT appears throughout the message flows as the communication/session terminator. Use the device protocol value expected by the serial API; in typical ASCII control-code handling this is 0x04.
3. LRC:
   - LRC is the XOR of every byte after the start delimiter (<STX> or <SI>) through and including the end delimiter (<ETX> or <SO>).
   - Do not include STX/SI in the XOR.
   - Do include ETX/SO in the XOR.
4. ACK/NAK/EOT handling:
   - After sending a frame, wait for ACK/NAK/EOT according to the command flow.
   - On NAK, retransmit the last frame subject to the documented retry behavior.
   - When receiving a device frame, validate LRC before ACK.
   - Send ACK for good LRC, NAK for bad LRC, EOT/cancel only where the flow allows it.
5. Android transport:
   - USB mode is CDC-ACM virtual COM. Manual lists USB 2.0 full speed, VID 0x6352, PID 0x294A.
   - RS232 default in the manual is 9600 bps, no parity, 8 data bits, 1 stop bit.
   - Keep transport isolated behind an interface so the same command code can work over USB serial, Bluetooth-serial adapters, or TCP bridges if needed.
6. Security:
   - Never log clear PIN, key material, clear PAN, full track data, encrypted key bundles, PIN blocks, or session keys.
   - Redact sensitive fields in debug logs.
   - Clear sensitive byte arrays after use where practical.
   - Keep PCI/PTS/SRED constraints in mind. The manual contains key management and account-data security notes that Codex must not bypass.
7. Data object notation:
   - <SUB> (0x1A) separates major parameters or data objects.
   - <FS> (0x1C) separates fields inside a data object.
   - Hex-string fields are ASCII characters representing hexadecimal bytes unless a field explicitly says binary/raw.
   - EMV data objects commonly use [Tag]<FS>[Format Code]<FS>[Value], with multiple objects separated by <SUB>.
8. Command modeling recommendation:
   - Create one command class per message ID or per request/response pair.
   - Include: id, frameType, request format, expected response id(s), field validators, and parser.
   - Use sealed result types for OK/fail/status/error results.
   - Preserve the message flow order for EMV transactions; invalid sequence can terminate the transaction.
9. Multi-packet support:
   - Implement generic packet sequencing for commands that send or receive multiple packets, including T01/T02, T03/T04, T05/T06, T2B/T2C, T2H/T2I, T33/T34, T51/T52, T55/T56, T59/T5A, T63/T64, T65/T66, JPEG transfers, and any command whose details mention packet type, sequence number, total packets, or control='0'.
10. Use the command details below as the authoritative implementation checklist. Do not infer missing field values. If a field is optional, only include it when the documented precondition applies.

Kotlin LRC reference:
fun calculateLrc(frameWithoutLrc: ByteArray): Byte {
    // frameWithoutLrc includes STX/SI at index 0 and includes ETX/SO at the end.
    var lrc = 0
    for (i in 1 until frameWithoutLrc.size) {
        lrc = lrc xor (frameWithoutLrc[i].toInt() and 0xFF)
    }
    return lrc.toByte()
}

Kotlin frame-building reference:
fun buildFrame(start: Byte, messageId: String, data: ByteArray, end: Byte): ByteArray {
    val idBytes = messageId.toByteArray(Charsets.US_ASCII)
    val noLrc = ByteArray(1 + idBytes.size + data.size + 1)
    noLrc[0] = start
    System.arraycopy(idBytes, 0, noLrc, 1, idBytes.size)
    System.arraycopy(data, 0, noLrc, 1 + idBytes.size, data.size)
    noLrc[noLrc.lastIndex] = end
    return noLrc + calculateLrc(noLrc)
}

MANUAL-DERIVED COMMAND DETAILS AND SUPPORTING REFERENCES

===== PDF PAGE 33 =====

Section 4            Message Format
    This chapter provides the details of the format of messages exchanged between the host application and
    the A10-P.


Notation Conventions
    The following conventions are used to make the description of messages more readable:


    Control Codes
    Control codes (non-displayable codes) are represented by two to three capital letters enclosed in angled
    brackets “<>“. This A10-P uses a total of 12 control codes. The actual code, when referenced, is
    represented by two hex digits enclosed in angled brackets, <0F> for example. The following table lists
    their usage and hex code values.


                 CODE       HEX VALUE                               USAGE

                   STX            02         Denotes the beginning of a message frame
                  ETX             03         Denotes the ending of a message frame
                  ACK             06         Acknowledge of message received
                   SO             0E         Denotes the ending of a message frame
                  NAK             15         Indicates invalid message received
                  SUB             1A         Message parameter follows
                   FS             1C         Field separator
                   GS             1D         Message ID follows
                   DC1            11         Used for Z2 message, enable the inverse mode.
                   DC2            12         Used for Z2 message, disable the inverse mode.


    [LRC]
    Each message frame transmitted is appended with an LRC byte to detect communication error. This byte
    should be regarded as part of the message frame but comes after the ending delimiter character. [LRC]
    is used in each message frame description to represent this LRC byte.
    LRC is calculated as an XORed value of every byte after the start code (<STX> or <SI>) in the message
    frame, all the way to and including the end code (<ETX> or <SO>).


    [item]
    A descriptive item name is enclosed in bracket. It denotes a string or data byte that has no fixed value.


===== PDF PAGE 34 =====

Message frame summary
    The data exchanged between the A10-P and the host is grouped into “message frames”. Each message
    frame has one of the two possible frame formats listed below:
    ⚫ <STX>[message ID][data]<ETX>[LRC]
    ⚫ <SI>[message ID][data]<SO>[LRC]
    Each type of message has a unique value in its message ID field. In the following texts, we refer a
    message type by its message ID value, e.g. “message 70”.


    Message type
    Messages exchanged between the A10-P and the host can be divided into two categories.
    One category is for administration and maintenance. In general administrative messages have the <SI>
    packet header and its return message sent back to the host is using the same message ID.
    The other category is for various transactions In general transaction messages are using the <STX>
    packet header. The messages work in pair. The odd number message sends the command and data to
    the A10-P, and the even number message returns the result.


    Time-out
    Whenever the A10-P sends out a message, a response (<ACK> character for acknowledgement or
    <NAK> character if LRC error occurs) from the host is expected. If the A10-P does not receive a
    response within 5 seconds, it will retransmit the last packet. If the A10-P does not receive <ACK> or
    <NAK> after two retransmit attempts, it will send out the <EOT> character and this communication
    session will be terminated.


    Transmission Error
    When the host decides that an invalid frame is received, it will send out the <NAK>. When the A10-P
    receives a <NAK>, it will retransmit its last message. If the message retransmitted is invalid again, then a
    <NAK> is sent by the host to request for another try. The A10-P will keep on retransmitting the message
    until an <ACK> or <EOT> is received.


    Packet Error
When the A10-P receives a good transmission but an invalid packet (wrong message id), it will ignore the
packet. If the received packet has an acceptable message id but having a wrong format, the A10-P will send
out <EOT> as the error message. It will send out more detailed error codes if the messages are PIN entry
functions.


===== PDF PAGE 35 =====

Section 5            Administration and Maintenance Messages

Message 02            Load Master Key (Clear Text)
    Format:           <SI>02[Key ID][Key value]<FS>[Usage][Mode]<SO>[LRC]
                      (request frame)
                      <SI>02?[error code]<SO>[LRC] (error frame)


    Message length: Variable (22 to 58 bytes).
    Usage:            Send the key value to the A10-P and save into designated master key slot. A10-P can
                      store 21 master keys (A10-P application only uses 16 of them for backword
                      compatibility); each with a one digit ID. These Master keys are divided into three
                      groups of different functions. Refer to Appendix A for key usage and ID definition.


                      Clear text key injection is only accepted in the “Key Injection” session of the system
                      manager; which is enabled by dual-password authentication. This function should be
                      used only in security-controlled environment with HSM. Refer to Appendix A for
                      detailed information.
                      The algorithm of the injected key is defined by the key management scheme, if the
                      usage and mode are not designated. A10-P will apply default value for them. Refer to
                      Appendix A for detailed information.
                      Change key management scheme (TDES) will erase all existing keys in the A10-P.
                      All existing keys in the A10-P will be erased when the first message 02 or 90 is sent in
                      a new key injection session.
    Compatibility:    The user of UIC PP790SE and PP791 shoud note:
                      1. The “key transport key” usage is changed to “K1” instead of “K0” in accordance to
                      the new ANSI TR-31: 2018 standard.


===== PDF PAGE 36 =====

    Message element:
                                          Request fame (HOST to A10-P)
                        Field           Length                 Value and description

                   <SI>               1             <0F>
                   02                 2             Message ID
                   [Key ID]           1             [‘0’ to ‘9’, ‘B’ to 'G']
                   [Key value]        16 or 32      Hexadecimal string for key value.
                                      or 48         In DES scheme, 16 characters (single length) .
                                                    In TDES scheme, 32 characters (double
                                                    length) or 48 characters (triple length) keys
                                                    are allowed.
                   <FS>               1             Field separator.
                                                    (Optional, only available in clear text format
                                                    frame if the following [Usage] and [Mode]
                                                    exist)
                   [Usage]            2             (Optional: ANSI TR-31 key usage for clear
                                                    text frame.)
                                                    Available values are:
                                                    “K0” for key encryption. (id 0 ~ 9, B ~ G)
                                                    “K1” for key transport key. (id F)
                                                    “P0” for PIN encryption. (id 0 ~ 9)
                                                    “M3” for MAC calculation. (id B ~ E)
                                                    “M1” for MAC calculation. (id B ~ E)
                                                    “D0” for data encryption. (id G)
                   [Mode]             1             (Optional: ANSI TR-31 key mode for clear
                                                    text frame.)
                                                    Available values are:
                                                    ‘D’ for decryption only. (K0 / K1 keys)
                                                    ‘E’ for encryption only (P0 / D0 keys)
                                                    ‘G’ for MAC generation only (M1 / M3 keys)
                                                    ‘V’ for MAC verification only (M3 keys)
                   <SO>               1             <0E>
                   [LRC]              1             Checksum

                         Field           Length                Value and description

                    <SI>                 1          <0F>
                    02                   2          Message ID
                    ?                    1
                    [Err msg]            1          ‘2’: Key duplicated with existing keys.
                                                    ‘3’: Internal fail: fail to allocate memory.
                                                    ‘4’: Internal fail: fail to read key structure.
                                                    ‘E’: Incompatible key usage.
                                                    ‘F’: Key injecton operating limit reached.
                    <SO>                 1          <0E>
                    [LRC]                1          Checksum


    Message flow:
                             HOST                 Direction                     A10-P

                    Message 02
                                                     →
                    (request frame)
                                                               <ACK> /<NAK>/<EOT>
                                                                Processing request.
                                                                If format error, send <EOT> and
                                                                end.
                                                     
                                                                If format and MAC correct, send
                                                                Message 02
                                                                (echo of request frame).
                    Verify echo frame.
                    If verify ok, send
                    <ACK>.
                    If packet LRC error, send
                                                     →
                    <NAK>.
                    If the host wants to
                    cancel the key loading
                    procedure, send <EOT>.
                                                                Save the key value and send
                                                     
                                                                <EOT>


===== PDF PAGE 38 =====

Message 02              Load Master Key (ETM format)
    Format:             <SI>02[Key ID][Key value (ETM format)]<SO>[LRC] (request frame)
                        <SI>02?[error code]<SO>[LRC] (error frame)


    Message length: 56 bytes
    Usage:
                The “ETM format” stands for “Encrypted, Tagged, and MACed” format, which is official format
                in PRC. In this format the key value has 50 characters: 32 for encrypted TDES key value, 2
                for Key type tag, and 16 for Message Authentication Code (MAC).
                Elements of ETM KEY format
                           Encrypted Key:32                             Key Type:2    Key MAC:16
                Available Key Type
                           00             Master keys for PIN entry. Available for position #0~#9, #F, #G
                           01             MAC master keys for MAC generation, Available for ID #B~#E
                           02             Session Key (Not available in A10-P)
                Generation of key MAC:
                Use specific key to perform ISO 9797-1, algorithm3 MAC calculation on encrypted key value,
                concatenated with key type and padding ‘0’. Key selection rule as follows:
                Key Encrypting Key and MAC key selecting rules
                           Key ID to be loaded             Key Encrypting Key           MAC Generating Key
                           0 to 9, F, G                    Key loading Key (Key #F)     Key loading Key (Key #F)
                           B to E                          Active Master Key            Active Master Key
                                                           (Key #0 to #9)               (Key #0 to #9)


                If message format or MAC value in the ETM format failed verification, A10-P will send
                <EOT> to end the key loading process.A10-P will send the whole message frame back to
                HOST as a confirmation of good reception. If host returns <EOT> as the response of echoed
                message, the key will not be saved.


    Message element:
                                          Request fame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                   Value and description

                   <SI>              1              <0F>
                   02                2              Message ID
                   [Key ID]          1              [‘0’ to ‘9’, ‘B’ to 'G']
                   [Key vaule]       50             Hexadecimal string for key value.
                                                    Encrypted key(ETM format): 50 characters.
                   <SO>              1              <0E>
                   [LRC]             1              Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 39 =====

                             Response fame – Error message (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field          Length                Value and description

                    <SI>              1            <0F>
                    02                2            Message ID
                    ?                 1
                    [Err msg]         1            ‘2’: Key duplicate.
                                                   ‘3’: Internal fail: fail to allocate memory.
                                                   ‘4’: Internal fail: fail to read key structure.
                                                   ‘7’: Fail to decrypt the key value.
                                                   ‘A’: Format error.
                                                   ‘B’: Insecure key inject. (New key is longer
                                                   than the key used to protect it.)
                                                   ‘C’: Fail to verify the MAC value.
                                                   ‘D’: KLK does not exist.
                                                   ‘E’: Incompatible key usage.
                                                   ‘F’: Key injecton operating limit reached.
                    <SO>              1            <0E>
                    [LRC]             1            Checksum
    Message flow:
                            HOST                   Direction                        A10-P

            Message 02 (request frame)                 →
                                                                <ACK> /<NAK>/<EOT>
                                                                 Processing request.
                                                                 If format error, send Message 02 (Error
                                                                message) and end.
                                                                 If format and MAC correct, send
                                                                 Message 02 (echo of request frame).
            Verify echo frame.
            If verify ok, send <ACK>.
            If packet LRC error, send <NAK>.           →
            If the host wants to cancel the key
            loading procedure, send <EOT>.
                                                                Save the key value and send <EOT>

Message 08            Select Active Master Key
    Format:            <SI>08[KeyID]<SO>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:             This message is used to select an “active” master key from one of the 10 possible PIN
                       encrypting master keys previously loaded using message 02. The active master key
                       will be used in the following transactions.


    Note:              Confirm the key existence before issuing 08: message 08 is not responsible to
                       check if [KeyID] has a valid master key. Use message 04 before message 08.


    Message element:
                                        Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                            Field         Length             Value and description

                     <SI>                1            <0F>
                     08                  2            Message ID
                     [KeyID]             1            0~9, one of Master key id.
                     <SO>                1            <0E>
                     [LRC]               1            Checksum


    Message flow:
                             HOST               Direction                  A10-P

                    Message 08                      →
                                                            <ACK>/<NAK>/<EOT>
                                                             Success:
                                                             <SI>080<SO>
                                                            Fail:
                                                             <SI>081<SO>
                                                             Key index > 9
                    <ACK> (Good echo)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                            <EOT>

Message 09              Communication Test
    Format:             <SI>09<SO>[LRC] (request frame)
                        <SI>09<SUB>PROCESSING<SO>[LRC] (response frame)
                        <SI>09[response code][LRC] (Result frame)


    Message length: Fixed 5 bytes for the requesting message, fixed 16 bytes for the response message.
    Usage:              This message is used to test communication link between the host and the A10-P.
                        Both host and A10-P can initiate a communication test. The initiating party should send
                        the requesting message; the other party should respond with the response message
                        that should be ACKed if received correctly. After verifying that the response message
                        is correct, the initiating party should send back the same response message and the
                        receiving party should acknowledge this message. The test results are shown on the
                        A10-P display.
    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <SI>              1             <0F>
                   09                2             Message ID
                   <SO>              1             <0E>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


                                       Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <SI>              1             <0F>
                   09                2             Message ID
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [Test string]     10            ASCII string “PROCESSING”
                   <SO>              1             <0E>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


                                          Result frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <SI>              1             <0F>
                   09                2             Message ID
                   [response         1             ‘0’: Test Success
                   code]                           ‘F’: Test Failed.
                   <SO>              1             <0E>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 48 =====

    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction                A10-P

                    Message 09 (request)           →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                          Message 09 (response frame)
                    <ACK> (Good echo)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                    Message 09 (response)          →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message 13            Adjust COM1 Baud Rate (RS-232 version only)
    Format:              <SI>13[baud code][mode]<SO>[LRC] (request frame)
                         <SI>13[status]<SO>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Variable, 6~7 bytes.
    Usage:               This message will change the communication baud rate and transmit mode of the
                         A10-P for later operations. The setting is kept in the non-volatile memory. The baud
                         rate will be changed after the message flow ends.
    Note:                If the [mode] parameter is not specified, the default transmit mode is N, 8, 1.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                             Field             Length              Value and description

                    <SI>                      1             <0F>
                    13                        2             Message ID
                    [baud code]               1             ASCII character
                                                            ‘1’ = 1200bps
                                                            ‘2’ = 2400bps
                                                            ‘3’ = 4800bps
                                                            ‘4’ = 9600bps
                                                            ‘5’ = 19200bps
                                                            ‘6’ = 38400bps
                                                            ‘7’ = 57600bps
                                                            ‘8’ = 115200bps
                    [Transmit mode]           1             ASCII character
                    (optional)                              ‘1’ = None parity, 8-bit, 1 stop bit
                                                            ‘2’ = Even parity, 7-bit, 1 stop bit
                                                            ‘3’ = Odd parity, 7-bit, 1 stop bit


                                                            0x41 = None parity, 8-bit, 1 stop bit,
                                                            with XON-XOFF flow control
                                                            0x42 = Even parity, 7-bit, 1 stop bit,
                                                            with XON-XOFF flow control
                                                            0x43 = Odd parity, 7-bit, 1 stop bit,
                                                            with XON-XOFF flow control


                                                            0x81 = None parity, 8-bit, 1 stop bit,
                                                            with RTS flow control
                                                            0x82 = Even parity, 7-bit, 1 stop bit,


===== PDF PAGE 53 =====

                                                                  with RTS flow control
                                                                  0x83 = Odd parity, 7-bit, 1 stop bit,
                                                                  with RTS flow control


                                                                  0xC1 = None parity, 8-bit, 1 stop bit,
                                                                  with RTS/XON-XOFF flow control
                                                                  0xC2 = Even parity, 7-bit, 1 stop bit,
                                                                  with RTS/XON-XOFF flow control
                                                                  0xC3 = Odd parity, 7-bit, 1 stop bit,
                                                                  with RTS/XON-XOFF flow control
                    <SO>                        1                 <0E>
                    [LRC]                       1                 Checksum


                                          Response frame (A10-P to HOST)

                    13                          2                 Message ID

                    [LRC]                       1                 Checksum

Section 6           Contact EMV Level 2 Transaction Messages
EMV Level2 transaction messages are divided into 2 groups. One group are the EMV-configuration data
operation messages (T01, T03, T05, T07, T09, T0B) and the other group are the EMV-transaction messages
(T11, T13, T15, T17, T19, T1C, T21, T25, T27, T29).


The issuing sequence of the ICC EMV transaction messages is controlled by the A10-P.                An invalid
sequence will terminate that ICC EMV transaction. In the beginning of an ICC EMV transaction, the user has
to issue message T11 to make the A10-P to negotiate with the card and generate a candidate list of
EMV-applications that are supported both by the A10-P and the card.       It will then automatically select the
application with the highest priority or the one selected by the user (according to the terminal configuration
data installed in the A10-P), and then return the EMV-application name. Message T15 is used for the
terminal-side to transmit transaction information such as the amount to be used by the A10-P to complete the
transaction with the card if the transaction does not need to be authorized online. The terminal can issue
message T1D to transmit additional transaction data to the A10-P for ICC EMV transaction.       Examples are
online response data and magnetic stripe card track data. Message T17 is applied if the transaction needs to
be authorized online. The terminal-side will transmit necessary information via this message to the A10-P to
complete the rest of transactional steps. If the response from the host contains issuer script (see EMV), the
terminal-side will issue message T19 to send these scripts into the A10-P. Then the A10-P will issue these
scripts at appropriate time to the card. Message T1C is used to terminate an ICC EMV transaction. Finally,
message T21 is used for terminal-side to get the transaction information throughout the EMV transaction.


The terminal can apply the same set of Txx messages for handling a complete ICC EMV transaction but for
turning out the transaction must be changed to magnetic stripe card processing. According to EMV rule, if the
terminal fails to read the IC card, the transaction could be switched to the magnetic stripe card transaction.
Because of the different types of magnetic stripe cards, the magnetic stripe card processing should be taken
by the terminal. The terminal can issue message Q1 provided by the A10-P to let the user to swipe his card
and then issue message 70 to complete a magnetic stripe card transaction. In this situation, the terminal will
get the T11 response message that indicates a failed IC card read. The terminal should then issue message
T1D, T15 and T17 to the A10-P for batch data capture. The flow chart for changing to magnetic stripe card
processing can be found in the “Overall Contact EMV level 2 transaction flow reference” section.


===== PDF PAGE 82 =====

    Error Code                                                Error Description

8000000E            Application not initial.

8000001D            Invalid amount.
8000001E            Amount overflow.

===== PDF PAGE 84 =====

Message T01                Terminal Configuration Setup
    Format:               <STX>T01[Pkt No.][Total Pkts]<SUB>[DO]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:                Host can use this command to send the terminal configuration data to the A10-P. This
                          command can be sent many times. The A10-P will save those data internally and
                          utilize them during the transaction. The A10-P will send message T02 (Terminal
                          Configuration Setup Response) to the host as a response. The functions of Message
                          T01 and Message T51 are exactly the same and can be used alternately.


    Message element:
                                            Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                           Field          Length                 Value and description

                     <STX>              1             <02>
                     T01                3             Message ID
                     Pkt No.            1             Decimal. Packet sequence number (1 ~ 9) (ex.
                                                      2)
                     Total Pkts         1             Decimal. Total packets (1 ~ 9) (ex. 8).
                     <SUB>              1             <1A>
                     DO                 Var.          Data Object, format as below
                     <ETX>              1             <03>
                     [LRC]              1             Checksum
    Data Object Format: each data object is delimited by <SUB>, and each field inside each data
    object is delimited by <FS>.


    Data Format: (Please also refer to EMV 4.3 BOOK3, section 4.3)
  Format                                                      Description
     1       a - Alphabetic data (a ~z, A~Z)
     2       b - unsigned binary numbers or bit combinations
     3       an - Alphanumeric data      (a ~z, A~Z, 0~9)
     4       ans - Alphanumeric Special data (Characters defined in ISO8859)
     5       cn - Compressed numeric data (0~9, left justify, pad hexadecimal ‘F’s. Ex. 12 34 56 12 3F FF)
     6       n - Numeric data (0~9, right justify, pad leading hexadecimal zeroes. Ex. 00 00 00 01 23 45)
     7       var - Variable data (Any bit combination)


   Note. User has to follow the restriction specified in the EMV 4.3 BOOK3, Annex A and Appendix C
           of this document to load the configuration data. The A10-P will check if the length of each
           configuration data item is consistent. Any inconsistent data item will make the data loading
           fail.


===== PDF PAGE 85 =====

    Note. If the data format is ‘2’ (binary), ‘5’ (compressed numeric), ‘6’ (numeric) or ‘7’(variable data),
           it cannot be allocated in message T01 directly. It should be transferred into a hexadecimal
           string and then allocated in message T01.


    Example: (Clear the terminal configuration data and then setup the new data.)
    Merchant Category Code: 0000 (Numerical)
    Terminal ID:           SmartPOS (Ascii)
    UI Capability:         0x01 (binary)


    <STX>T0111<SUB>9f15<FS>6<FS>0000<SUB>9f1c<FS>3<FS>SmartPOS<SUB>
    FFFF8101<FS>2<FS>01<ETX>[LRC]


    Special Tag for A10: 0xFFFF8101, 0xFFFF81013
            Name                             Description                        Format        Tag             Length
      Terminal UI          0: Make A10-P selects the highest priority             b      FFFF8101         1
      Capability           application and ask for the user’s confirmation.
                           1: Make A10-P provides a list of candidate
                           applications.
      MSR Processing       A10-P will store transaction data according to         b      FFFF81013        var
      Batch Tag List       the tag list while the transaction is performed as
                           MSR processing. Ex, for tag list (829F365F3
                           09F399F029F03), the A10-P will store the
                           transaction data of tag numbers 0x82, 0x9F36,
                           0x5F30, 0x9F39, 0x9F02 and 0x9F03.
    These data objects are defined in EMV 4.3 BOOK3, Annex A without tag values. The tag values are
    defined by UIC.


    The A10-P will check if the terminal downloads the minimum set of terminal-related information
    into itself. The download process will fail if there is not enough data in this message. Please refer
    to Appendix D for the minimum set of terminal-related data

===== PDF PAGE 88 =====

Message T03                Certification Authority Public Key Setup
    Format:           Plain Text (Key Format: 1)
                      <STX>T03[Op code][RID][PKI][Hash Algo][Hash][PK Algo]
                      [PK Leng] [PK Exponent]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)
                      <STX>T03[Op code][PK Modulus]<ETX> (request frame)


    Format:           Certificate (Key Format: 2(PEM) or 3(DER))
                      <STX>T03[PktType][SeqNo]<SUB>[RID][PKI]<FS>[Cert]<ETX>[LRC]
                      (request frame)
                      <STX>T03[PktType][SeqNo]<SUB>[Cert]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            Host can use this command to send the Certification Authority Public key data to the
                      A10-P. Each command can only setup one key but this command can be sent many
                      times. The A10-P will save those key data inside and use those data during the
                      transaction. A10-P will send message T04 (Certification Authority Public Key Setup
                      Response) to the host. The data is installed into the A10-P via this message and it will
                      then be saved into its internal storage structure with a name as the concatenation of
                      the values in the [RID] and [PKI] fields. For example, the value in the [RID] field is
                      “A000000003” and the value in the [PKI] field is “90”, then the A10-P will save these
                      data and give an ID as “A00000000390”. The functions of Message T03 and Message
                      T53 are exactly the same and can be used alternately.
    Key Format:       There are three types of key format. ‘1’ is plain text format, which is [RID][PKI][Hash
                      Algo][Hash][PK Algo][PK Leng][PK Exponent][PK Modulus]. ‘2’ is PEM certificate
                      format, which is [RID][PKI][PEM certificate]. ‘3’ is DER certificate format, which is
                      [RID][PKI][DER certificate with HEX String format].


===== PDF PAGE 89 =====

    Message element:
    1st Packet (Plain Text Public Key):
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T03               3             Message ID
                   [Op code]         1             1: Load first part of RSA public key
                   [RID]             10            Hexadecimal string, the left 5 bytes of
                                                   Registered Application Provider ID
                   [PKI]             2             Public Key Index, hexadecimal string. (Refer
                                                   to EMV 4.3, tag ‘9F22’)
                   [Hash             2             Hash Algorithm Index, hexadecimal string
                   Algorithm]                      ‘01’: SHA-1. Now, A10-P accepts only ‘01’.
                   [Hash]            40            Hash checksum, hexadecimal
                                                   SHA1(RID+PKI+PKModules+PKExp)
                   [PK               2             Public Key Algorithm, hexadecimal string
                   Algorithm]                      ‘01’: RSA digital signature. Now, A10-P only
                                                   accepts ‘01’.
                   [PK Leng]         2             Public Key Modulus length, hexadecimal
                                                   string, for example:
                                                   ‘80’ = 128 bytes = 1024 bits
                   [PK Exponent]     1             Public Key Exponent
                                                   ‘1’: 3
                                                   ‘2’: 216+1
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 90 =====

    2nd Packet (Plain Text Public Key):
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T03               3             Message ID
                   [Op code]         1             2: Load second part of RSA public key
                   [PK Modulus]      Var           Public    Key       Modulus,    presented       in
                                                   hexadecimal, data length = 2*[PK Leng bytes]
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


    PEM certificate:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T03               3             Message ID
                   [PktType]         1             ‘0’: Initial or middle packet of CA public
                                                   keys.
                                                   ‘1’: Single or last packet of CA public keys.
                   [SeqNo]           1             The packet sequence number, starting from
                                                   ‘0’.
                   <SUB>             1             <1A> as delimiter
                   [RID]             10            Hexadecimal string, the left 5 bytes of
                                                   Registered Application Provider ID
                   [PKI]             2             Public Key Index, hexadecimal string. (Refer
                                                   to EMV 4.3, tag ‘9F22’)
                   <FS>              1             <1C> as delimiter
                   [certificate]     Var.          Base64 encoded string.
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 91 =====

    DER certificate:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field         Length                Value and description

                    <STX>             1             <02>
                    T03               3             Message ID
                    [PktType]         1             ‘4’: Initial or middle packet of CA public
                                                    keys.
                                                    ‘5’: Single or last packet of CA public keys.
                    [SeqNo]           1             The packet sequence number, starting from
                                                    ‘0’.
                    <SUB>             1             <1A> as delimiter
                    [RID]             10            Hexadecimal string, the left 5 bytes of
                                                    Registered Application Provider ID
                    [PKI]             2             Public Key Index, hexadecimal string. (Refer
                                                    to EMV 4.3, tag ‘9F22’)
                    <FS>              1             <1C> as delimiter
                    [certificate]     Var.          DER certificate content with Base64 encoded
                                                    string format.
                    <ETX>             1             <03>
                    [LRC]             1             Checksum
    Message flow:
                              HOST               Direction                    A10-P

                    1st Message T03                  →
                                                               <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                              <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                               <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              1st Response Message T04
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                  →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                    2nd Message T03                  →
                                                               <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                              <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                               <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              2nd Response Message T04
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                  →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message T05                EMV Application Configuration Setup
    Format:           <STX>T05[Pkt No.][Total Pkts]<SUB>[AID]<SUB>[DO]<ETX>[LRC]
                      (request frame)
                      <STX>T05[Pkt No.][Total Pkts]<SUB>[DO]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            Host can use this command to send out the EMV application configuration data to the
                      A10-P. This command can be sent many times but each command is only used for one
                      application. A10-P will save those data internally and use those data during the
                      transaction. A10-P will send message T06 (EMV Application Configuration Setup
                      Response) to the host. For the data installed into A10-P via this message will be saved
                      in the internal storage structure with a name same as in the [AID] field.
    Message element:
    1st Message:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T05               3             Message ID
                   [Pkt No.]         1             Decimal. Packet sequence number (1)
                   [Total Pkts]      1             Decimal. Total packets (1 ~ 9) (ex. 8).
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [AID]             10~32         EMV Application ID, refer to EMV 4.3
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [DO]              Var.          Data Object, format as below
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum
    Rest of Message (If there are 2 more messages):
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T05               3             Message ID
                   [Pkt No.]         1             Decimal. Packet sequence number (2 ~ 9) (ex.
                                                   2).
                   [Total Pkts]      1             Decimal. Total packets (2 ~ 9) (ex. 8).
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [DO]              Var.          Data Object, format as below
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 94 =====

    Data Format: (Please also refer to EMV 4.3 BOOK3, section 4.3)
     Format                                               Description
        1     a - Alphabetic data (a ~z, A~Z)
        2     b - unsigned binary numbers or bit combinations
        3     an - Alphanumeric data (a ~z, A~Z, 0~9)
        4     ans - Alphanumeric Special data (Characters defined in ISO8859)
        5     cn - Compressed numeric data (0~9, left justify, pad hexadecimal ‘F’s. Ex. 12 34 56 12 3F FF)
        6     n - Numeric data (0~9, right justify, pad leading hexadecimal zeroes. Ex. 00 00 00 01 23 45)
        7     var - Variable data (Any bit combination)


Note. User has to follow the restriction specified in EMV 4.3 BOOK3, Annex A and Appendix C of this
      document to load the configuration data. A10-P will check if the length of each configuration
      data item is consistent. Any inconsistent data item will make the data loading fail.
Note. If the data format is ‘2’ (binary), ‘5’ (compressed numeric), ‘6’ (numeric) or ‘7’ (variable data), it
      cannot be allocated in message T05 directly. It should be transferred into a hexadecimal string
      and then allocated in message T05.
Example:
Default TDOL: 97 07 9f 02 06 95 05 9b 02 (binary)
Threshold Value for Biased Random Selection: 00 00 00 00 40 00(numerical)
Max. Target percentage to be used for Biased Random selection: 100 (decimal) / 0x46 (binary)


      <STX>T0511<SUB>A00000031010<SUB>97<FS>2<FS>97079f020695059b02
      <SUB>40000004<FS>6<FS>000000004000 <SUB>40000006<FS>2<FS>46<ETX>[LRC]
A10-P saves these data and give an ID as “A00000031010” to this group of data.


===== PDF PAGE 95 =====

0x40000009.
                      Name                           Description   Format        Tag           Length
      Application Selection Indicator        See below               n      40000001      1
      Threshold Value for Biased Random      See below               n      40000004      6
      Selection
      Target Percentage to be used for Biased See below              b      40000005      1
      Random Selection
      Maximum Target Percentage to be used See below                 b      40000006      1
      for Biased Random Selection
      Terminal Action Code - Default         See below               b      40000007      5
      Terminal Action Code - Denial          See below               b      40000008      5
      Terminal Action Code - Online          See below               b      40000009      5
      Data Tags required in Online message See below                 b      4000000A      var.
      (ARQC)
      Data tags required in reversal message See below               b      4000000D      var.
      Data tags for batch data capture       See below               b      40000010      var.
      ARC Approve                            See below               b      4000001A      var.
      ARC Decline                            See below               b      4000001B      var.
      ARC Referral                           See below               b      4000001C      var.
    These data objects are defined in EMV 4.3 BOOK3, Annex A without tag values. The tag values are
    defined by the A10-P.

Message T07                Data Format Table Setup
    Format:             <STX>T07[Clear]<SUB>[DO]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:              A10-P will check the consistency of the TLV object from the terminal via messages
                        T01 and T05. The terminal can add more TLV format checking rules into the A10-P.
                        A10-P will send message T08 (Data Format Setup Response) to the host as the
                        response.


    Message element:
                                            Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                           Field          Length                   Value and description

                     <STX>              1             <02>
                     T07                3             Message ID
                     [Clear]            1             ‘1’: Clear existing Data Format file
                     <SUB>              1             <1A>, Optional, only if [DO] exist
                     [DO]               Var.          Data Object, format as below
                     <ETX>              1             <03>
                     [LRC]              1             Checksum


    Format of Data Objects:
     The Data Objects for T07 shall include five data fields delimited by <FS>: [Tag#] <FS> [Format] [Min
    Len] [Max Len] [Flag]. Each data object is delimited by a <SUB> to construct multiple <DO>s. If the [Flag]
    is ‘0’, the data object will accept the length of value within [Min Len] and [Max Len]; if the [Flag] is ‘1’, the
    data object will only accept either the [Min Len] or [Max Len] as valid length.


    Example: (setup new data format.)


              Tag          Data Format         Min Len Max Len Or Flag

              5F88                 4             02           1A     0 (data length is 2 ~ 26 bytes)
              5F99                 2             08           0B     1 (data length is 8 bytes or 11 bytes)


    <STX>T071<SUB>5F88<FS>4021A0<SUB>5F99<FS>2080B1<ETX>[LRC]


    In this example, A10-P will check if the length of data with tag value 5F99 is 8-byte or 11-byte. It will
    check if the length of data with tag value 5F88 is more than 1-byte and less than 27-byte.

                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)

                                                          Message T08
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message T0A                Response of EMV Config Data Query Message
    Format:           <STX>T0A[Config Type][Status]<SUB>[ID List]<ETX>[LRC]
                      (response frame)

                                        Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field          Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>              1             <02>
                   T0A                3             Message ID
                   Config Type        1             Return the input data in the field [Config
                                                    Type] of T09.
                   Status             1             0: There is data in identified data type.
                                                    1: No data in identified data type.

    Ex. [ID List] = A00000000390<FS> A000000004F8<FS> A000000004F5 (Config Type = 1)

Message T0B                Delete EMV Configuration Data Message.
    Format:            <STX>T0B[Config Type]<SUB>[ID List]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             Host can use this command to delete the EMV application configuration data or the CA
                       public key in the A10-P. This command can be sent many times.


    Message element:
                                           Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length                Value and description

                    <STX>              1           <02>
                    T0B                3           Message ID
                    [Config Type]      1           1: CA public key.
                                                   2: EMV application data.
                    <SUB>              1           <1A>
                    [ID List]          Var         List of IDs that will be erased.
                    <ETX>              1           <03>
                    [LRC]              1           Checksum


    [ID List]: The concatenation of IDs. There is a <FS> between each ID.
    Ex. [ID List] = A00000000390<FS> A000000004F8<FS> A000000004F5 (Config Type = 1 )
        [ID List] = A0000000041010<FS>B0123456781234<FS> A000000010 ( Config Type = 2 )


    Message flow:
                                HOST             Direction                    A10-P

                    Message T0B                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                             Message T0C
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 104 =====

Message T0C                Response of Delete EMV Configuration Data Message.
    Format:            <STX>T0C[Config Type][Status]<SUB>[Del Result]<ETX>[LRC]
                       (response frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             The response message of message T0B.


    Message element:
                                          Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field           Length                 Value and description

                    <STX>               1             <02>
                    T0C                 3             Message ID
                    [Config Type]       1             1: CA public key.
                                                      2: EMV application data.
                    [Status]            1             0: Delete OK
                                                      1: Fatal error.
                                                      2: Format error.
                                                      3: No CA public key or EMV application data
                                                      exists.
                                                      4: Sequence error.
                    <SUB>               1             <1A>, Optional, if Status is 0.
                    [Del Result]        Var.          Optional, if Status is 0.
                                                      0: the ID deletion is successful.
                                                      1: the ID deletion is failure.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum


    [Del Result]: It’s the concatenation of the results for the delete operation on the indicated ID. There is a
    <FS> between each result. If the delete operation is successful (when A10-P finds out the matched one
    and delete it successfully), the result is 0. If there is no such ID as the terminal specified, the result is 1.


    Ex. Terminal issues message T0B to erase the EMV application data with ID “A0000000102020”,
    “A0000020” and “A0000000103030”. Now the A10-P keeps the EMV application data with ID
    “A0000000103030” only.
    The data in the [Del Result] field will be “1<FS>1<FS>0”.


    Message flow:      Please refer to message T0B.


===== PDF PAGE 105 =====

Message T11                Application Select
    Format:            <STX>T11<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:             A10-P performs an application select on the active smart card. A10-P will prompt the
                       user to insert its card if the card has not yet be engaged. A10-P will send message T12
                       (Application Selection Response) to host as a response.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length               Value and description

                    <STX>            1             <02>
                    T11              3             Message ID
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                              HOST              Direction                   A10-P

                    Message T11                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              Application Select Response.
                                                    
                                                              Message T12
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 106 =====

Message T12                Application Select Response
    Format:           <STX>T12[Status][Application Name]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)
                      <STX>T12[Status][Reason][ErrMessage]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Variable, depending on the length of returned application name.
    Usage:            The message contains the name of the final selected application on the smart card to
                      be sent to the host. In general, the application name conforms to the EMV 4.3 level 2
                      specification. Refer to these standards for more detailed information.


    Message element:
                                       Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field         Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T12               3             Message ID
                   Status            1             0:OK
                   [Application      10-32         EMV application is selected successfully.
                   Name]                           Format: Hexadecimal string. The content of
                                                   this field does not exist if no EMV application
                                                   is selected.
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


    OR


                                       Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field         Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T12               3             Message ID
                   Status            1             1:Fail
                   [Reason]          1             1:Fatal Error
                                                   2:Command Format Error
                                                   3:Transaction Canceled
                                                   4: MSR processing.
                   [Err Message]     8             Optional, if Reason = ‘1’Hex String
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 107 =====

    NOTE. According to the EMV Level2’s rule, if the IC card cannot be read, A10-P will return message
            “T1214” to indicate that the terminal should apply magnetic stripe card processing. In this
            situation, the terminal should issue message Q1 to get the magnetic stripe data and issue
            message T1D to inform the A10-P on the magnetic stripe card data element. Message T15 is
            used to transmit the necessary data records (amount, transaction type, and so on...) for the
            A10-P to complete this transaction.


    Message flow:     Please refer to message T11.


===== PDF PAGE 108 =====

    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.

                       message T16), the terminal can issue this message to ask the A10-P to display the
                       rest of the EMV applications for cardholder selection.

                    [LRC]            1             Checksum

                    Message T13                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              Application Select Response.
                                                    
                                                              Message T12
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 109 =====

Message T15                Start Transaction
    Format:           <STX>T15<SUB>[AmtAuth]<SUB>[AmtOther]<SUB>
                      [CurExponent][CurCode]<SUB>[TranType]<SUB>[TranInfo]<SUB>
                      [Account Type]<SUB>[Force Online]<SUB>
                      [Encrypted Session key]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            A10-P performs a completed EMV transaction flow (via ‘Initiate Application’ through
                      ‘Completion’, see EMV 4.3, book 3, chap 8.2, figure 6 - transaction flow example)
                      based on the selected EMV application that has the corrected application name on the
                      T12 (Application Select response). The A10-P will prompt the user to arrange the
                      appropriate entries when informed, like confirmation or enter the PIN code. A10-P will
                      send message T16 (Start Transaction Response) to the host as a response. If the IC
                      card cannot be read (switch to magnetic stripe card processing), the A10-P will
                      arrange the information provided from this message and add the magnetic stripe card
                      data       to finish the EMV transaction (The rest of operation on magnetic stripe card is
                      outside the scope of EMV). The terminal could have extra operation on magnetic stripe
                      card transaction (by issuing message 70 for PIN, and so on.).


    Message element:
                                           Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field          Length                Value and description

                   <STX>               1           <02>
                   T15                 3           Message ID
                   <SUB>               1           <1A>
                   [AmtAuth]           12          Hexadecimal, Amount Authorized tag
                                                   ‘0x9F02’
                   <SUB>               1           <1A>
                   [AmtOther]          12          Hexadecimal, Amount Other, tag ‘0x9F03’
                   <SUB>               1           <1A>
                   [CurExponent]       1           Hexadecimal, tag ‘0x5F36’
                   [CurCode]           3           Hexadecimal, tag ‘0x5F2A’.
                                                   For example, USD$ = 0x840
                   <SUB>               1           <1A>
                   [TranType]          2           Hexadecimal, Transaction Type, tag ‘0x9c’
                   <SUB>               1           <1A>
                   [TranInfo]          2           Transaction Info, tag ‘0x60000001’
                   <SUB>               1           <1A>
                   [Account            2           Account Type, tag ‘0x5F57’


===== PDF PAGE 110 =====

                    Type]
                    <SUB>            1             <1A>
                    [Force Online]   1             ‘1’: Force this transaction to be authorized
                                                   online.   Valid only if the terminal
                                                   configuration enables the capability to support
                                                   online authorization.
                                                    ‘0’: Run the default EMV authorization flow
                    <SUB>            1             <1A> (Optional, if the Encrypted Session key
                                                   exists)
                    [Encrypted       16 or 32      (Optional, if the transaction needs the user to
                    Session key]     or 48         enter the PIN for online authorization. If no
                                                   session key exists, the A10-P will use PIN
                                                   encryption key(Usage P0) or DUKPT key
                                                   exist in A10-P). Only TDES session key are
                                                   used for EMV online PIN entry.
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                             HOST               Direction                    A10-P

                    Message T15                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              Start the Transaction Response.
                                                    
                                                              Message T16
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 111 =====

Message T16                Transaction Response
    Format:           <STX>T16[Status][Reason][Err Message][Result]<SUB>
                      <Advice Need><Reversal Need><Financial Need><ETX>[LRC]
                      (response frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            The message contains the transaction result on the smart card to be sent to terminal.


    Message element:
                                         Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field           Length                  Value and description

                   <STX>               1             <02>
                   T16                 3             Message ID
                   [Status]            1             0:OK; 1:Fail
                   [Reason]            1             Optional. (If Status = 1)
                                                     1:Fatal Error
                                                     2:Command Format Error
                                                     3:Transaction Cancel
                   [Err Message]       8             Optional, if Reason = ‘1’Hex String
                   [Result]            2             Optional. (If Status = 0)
                                                     ‘Y1’: Offline Approved,
                                                     ‘Z1’: Offline Declined
                                                     ‘Y3’: Unable to go online,
                                                             Offline Approved
                                                     ‘Z3’: Unable to go online, Offline Decline.
                                                     ‘Y4’: Online Approved
                                                     ‘Z4’: Online Decline
                                                     ‘A1’: Online Authorize Request,
                                                     ‘A4’: Application reselection.
                   <Reversal           1             0: Terminal does not need to send a reversal to
                   Need>                             the host for this transaction
                                                     1: Terminal should send a reversal to the host
                                                     for this transaction.
                   <ETX>               1             <03>
                   [LRC]               1             Checksum


    Note: If the [Result] is ‘A1’, then the terminal should send the online authorization request to the issuer
           host.   After that is done, send message T17 to the A10-P to continue the transaction. (See EMV
           4.3, book 3, chap 9, figure 7)


===== PDF PAGE 112 =====

    Note: If the transaction is switched to MSR processing, T16 will always return “A1” in the [Result] field.


===== PDF PAGE 113 =====

    Note: If the previous selected application on the IC card cannot perform the transaction (for example, this
           application has blocked) but has another application ID within this IC card, the A10-P will respond
           ‘A4’ to let the terminal knows and the terminal can issue message T13 to select another
           application ID and issue message T15 to re-start the transaction. Please refer to the paragraph of
           “Ref. 5 Packet command flow for first EMV application is blocked” in the section of “Overall EMV
           Level 2 transaction flow reference”.


    Message flow:
        Refer to message T15.


===== PDF PAGE 114 =====

Message T17                Send Online Authorized Code
    Format:           <STX>T17[OnlineRes]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)
                      <STX>T17[OnlineRes]<SUB>[ARC]<SUB>[IAD]<ETX>[LRC]
                      (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            A10-P continues to perform the EMV transaction flow after receiving this online
                      response from the host when the transaction response in message T16 is ‘A1’ (online
                      authorized request, see EMV 4.3, book 3, chap 8.2, figure 6 – transaction flow
                      example). A10-P will send the message T16 (Start Transaction Response) to the host.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T17               3             Message ID
                   Online Res        1             ‘0’: Unable to go online
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


    OR
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T17               3             Message ID
                   Online Res        1             ‘1’: Get Online Response,
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   ARC               2             Optional, if there is ARC from host.
                                                   Authorisation Response code, ASCII (0~9,
                                                   A~Z),.
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [IAD]             16~32         Optional, if there is IAD from host.
                                                   Issuer Authentication Data , Hex string.
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 115 =====

    OR (If the transaction is changed to Magnetic stripe card processing)
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length               Value and description

                    <STX>            1             <02>
                    T17              3             Message ID
                    Online Res       1             ‘3’: MSR Online Approve
                                                   Other: MSR Online Decline,
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Note: If [Online Res] is ‘4’, then A10-P will decide to approve or decline by the rule of TAC/IAC default.


    Message flow:
                              HOST              Direction                   A10-P

                    Message T17                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              Start Transaction Response.
                                                    
                                                              Message T16
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
