Message T19                  Send Issuer Script Command
    Format:                <STX>T19[IS]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:                 A10-P performs the Issuer script processing as in the EMV transaction flow. After
                           receiving this command from the host as the response message upon performing
                           online authorization. This command can be sent many times if too many script
                           commands needed to be processed. But the last one should be sent before message
                           T17.


    Message element:
                                                Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                            Field              Length                    Value and description

                     <STX>                  1              <02>
                     T19                    3              Message ID
                     [IS]                   Var.           Issuer Script, format as below. Hex string.
                     <ETX>                  1              <03>
                     [LRC]                  1              Checksum


    Message flow:
                                   HOST                    Direction                      A10-P

                         Message T19                            →
                                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                             Message T20
                         <ACK> (Good LRC)
                         <NAK> (Bad LRC)                        →
                         <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


    Issuer Script Format: (see EMV 4.3, book 3, chap 10, Figure 14)
          T                         L                      T         L              Script ID                 Commands
     ‘71’ or ‘72’               Including                ‘9F18’     ‘04’       Identifier (4 bytes)     Issuer Script Command
                         Script ID, tags, lengths                                                               Format
                                                                                                              (see below)


    Issuer Script Command Format: (see EMV 4.3, book 3, chap 10.10, Figure 15)
         T1         L1        V1        T2          L2         V2        T3        L3       V3        Tx       Lx        Vx
        ‘86’    L(V1)        cmd        ‘86’       L(V2)    cmd        ‘86’      L(V3)      cmd       ‘86’    L(Vx)     cmd

Message T1D                Transaction/Configuration Data Overwrite
    Format:             <STX>T1D<SUB>[DO]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:              The terminal can use this command to update transaction/configuration data of the
                        A10-P in run time. This command can be sent multiple times if needed. A10-P will save
                        those data internally and use those data during the transaction. As a response the
                        A10-P will send the message T1E to the terminal.
    [Notes]
                      1. T1D will temporarily override the transaction/configuration data (which were set by
                           the previous configuration commands) within this transaction. Those previously
                           configurations will be resumed in the new transaction.
                      2. Any erroneous transaction data update may fail the transaction process.
                      3. For the issuer returned authorization data such as ARC, IAD, or issuer scripts should
                           use message T17 to send to the A10-P in order to complete the EMV transaction.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T1D               3             Message ID
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>, Optional, only if [DO] exists
                   DO                Var.          Data Object, format as below
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


    Data Object:
        Each <DO> shall include three data fields: [Tag#] || [Format] || [Value], and each field shall delimit
    with a <FS>. Each data object is delimited by a <SUB> to construct multiple <DO>s. The [tag#] defined
    in EMV 4.3 Book3 Annex A and specific [tag#] defined at Appendix C of this manual have the
    pre-defined data format and length range. These [tag#] must follow up with these rules, otherwise the
    A10-P will reject this data setup. Any customer defining [tag#] number shall use message T07 to
    pre-define the data format and length range before using message T1D to load the data.


===== PDF PAGE 120 =====

    Data Format: (Please also refer to EMV 4.3 BOOK3, section 4.3)
     Format                                                  Description
        1      a - Alphabetic data (a ~z, A~Z)
        2      b - unsigned binary numbers or bit combinations
        3      an - Alphanumeric data    (a ~z, A~Z, 0~9)
        4      ans - Alphanumeric Special data (Characters defined in ISO8859)
        5      cn - Compressed numeric data (0~9, left justify, pad hexadecimal ‘F’s. Ex. 12 34 56 12 3F FF)
        6      n - Numeric data (0~9, right justify, pad leading hexadecimal zeroes. Ex. 00 00 00 01 23 45)
        7      var - Variable data (Any bit combination)


    Example:
    (If the IC card cannot be read, the terminal will switch to MSR processing and then load these data into
    the A10-P.)
    Service Code:          0211 (Numeric)
    PAN:          123456789022334455(Compressed Numeric)
    Terminal sends,
    <STX>T1D<SUB>5F30<FS>6<FS>0211<SUB>5A<FS>5<FS>123456789022334455<ETX>[LRC]


    Message flow:
                             HOST                Direction                   A10-P

                    Message T1D                      →
                                                                 <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                                <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                 <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                Message T1E
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                  →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 121 =====

Message T25                Get Batch Data
    Format:           <STX>T25<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:            This message is used to get the batch data (EMV).


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length              Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T25               3             Message ID
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


    In each issuing of message T25, the A10-P will return one record in the batched data via message T26.
    The terminal can keep issuing message T25 until the A10-P returns a message to indicate that there is
    no more record in the batch data.


    User can load a tag list of batch data into the A10-P, and the A10-P will search the corresponding values
    according to the tag list and store it as a transaction record. Please refer to Appendix C for more details.


    Note. If there is no more memory space for batch data, the EMV transaction will turn out to be failed.
    Please issue message T23 to release the memory space.
    Note. A10-P can store at most 1000 transaction records.


===== PDF PAGE 126 =====

    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction                A10-P

                    Message T25                    →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                          Message T26 (T262)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                    Message T25                    →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                          Message T26 (T262)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                    …                              …       …
                    Message T25                    →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                          Message T26 (T261)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)                       A10-P erases batch data.
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 127 =====

Message T26                Response of Get Batch Data message
    Format:            <STX>T26[Status][record of Batch data]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             Return the batch data to the terminal.


    Message element:
                                        Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field         Length                 Value and description

                    <STX>             1             <02>
                    T26               3             Message ID
                    Status            1             0: No more record.
                                                    1: It is the last record inside the A10-P.
                                                    2: There is still record existing inside the
                                                    A10-P.
                    [Batch data]      Var.          Hex string.
                    <ETX>             1             <03>
                    [LRC]             1             Checksum


    Format of Batch data:
    According to the tag list of the batch data (refer to Appendix C), A10-P will search the corresponding
    values    of the tag list and store it as a transaction record.
    [EMV Tag Number (1 ~ 4 byte)] || [Length (1byte)] || [Value] || [EMV Tag Number (1 ~ 4 byte)] || [Length
    (1byte)] || [Value] || …[EMV Tag Number (1 ~ 4 byte)] || [Length (1byte)] || [Value]. A10-P can save             up
    to 256 bytes of data as one record. The right part of data will be ignored if the length record is greater
    than 256 bytes.


    A10-P will unpack the binary data into hex string and return to the terminal. For example, if the data of
    one record is 0x9F02 || 06 || 000000001100 || 5A || 10 || 1122334455667 7889900AABBCCDDEEFF,
    then the terminal will see “9F02060000000011005A1011223344556677889900A
    ABBCCDDEEFF” in the [Batch data] field.


    Message flow: Please refer to message T25


===== PDF PAGE 128 =====

Message T27                Get Online authorization Data
    Format:            <STX>T27<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:             Issue this message to get the (EMV) data for online authorization.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length                Value and description

                    <STX>            1             <02>
                    T27              3             Message ID
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    While the EMV transaction must be authorized online, message T27 is issued to get the necessary data
    for online authorization from the A10-P. A10-P will wait for the terminal to send message T17 as to
    inform the go online authorization result.


    During the application configuration setup (using message T05) the user shall load a data object’s tag list
    in tag# 4000000A into the A10-P to be used as the online authorization data. After receiving message
    T27 from the terminal, A10-P will search the corresponding values according to this tag list and return
    that online authorization data with message T28 . Please refer to Appendix C for more details.


    Message flow:
                              HOST               Direction                  A10-P

                    Message T27                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                             Message T28
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 129 =====

Message T28                Response of Get Online authorization Data message
    Format:           <STX>T28[online authorization data]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            Return the online authorization data to the terminal.


    Message element:
                                       Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field        Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T28               3             Message ID
                   Online            Var.          Hex string.
                   authorization                   Optional, if this transaction needs to be
                   data                            authorized online.
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


    Online authorization data:
    According to the tag list of online authorization data (refer to Appendix C), A10-P will search the
    corresponding values according to the tag list and return it as the transaction record.
    [EMV Tag Number (1 ~ 4 byte)] || [Length (1byte)] || [Value] || [EMV Tag Number (1 ~ 4 byte)] || [Length
    (1byte)] || [Value] || …[EMV Tag Number (1 ~ 4 byte)] || [Length (1byte)] || [Value]. A10-P can return          up
    to 256 bytes of data as one record. The right part of data will be ignored if the length record is greater
    than 256 bytes.


    A10-P will unpack the binary data into hex string and return to the terminal. For example, the data of one
    record is 0x9F02 || 06 || 000000001100 || 5A || 10 || 11223344556677889900AABBC
    CDDEEFF,                        the                      terminal                          will                see
    “9F02060000000011005A1011223344556677889900AABBCCDDEEFF” in the [Batch data] field.


    Message flow: Please refer to message T27

Message T29                Get Reversal Data
    Format:            <STX>T29<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:             Issue this message to get the (EMV) data for reversal.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length               Value and description

                    <STX>            1             <02>
                    T29              3             Message ID
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    If the EMV transaction needs a reversal, issuing message T29 to get the necessary data from the A10-P
    for reversal. User can load a tag list for reversal data into the A10-P and the latter will search the
    corresponding values according to the tag list and return it as a transaction record. Please refer to
    Appendix C for more details.


    Message flow:
                              HOST              Direction                   A10-P

                    Message T29                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                             Message T2A
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


    Note. Terminal can always get reversal data even if message T16 indicates that the reversal is not
    needed.

    [EMV Tag Number (1 ~ 4 byte)] || [Length (1byte)] || [Value] || [EMV Tag Number (1 ~ 4 byte)] || [Length
    (1byte)] || [Value] || …[EMV Tag Number (1 ~ 4 byte)] || [Length (1byte)] || [Value]. A10-P can return uo to

Message T2B                Get Online authorization Data with multi packets
    Format:           <STX>T2B[control]<ETX>[LRC]                 (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 or 7 bytes.
    Usage:            Issue this message to get the (EMV) data for online authorization.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T2B               3             Message ID
                   [control]         1             Optional, as acknowledgment of multiple
                                                   packets
                                                   ‘0’ = ready for next packet
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


    If the EMV transaction must be authorized online, issue message T2B from the A10-P to get the
    necessary data for online authorization. If the transaction must be authorized online, the user can load a
    tag list of online authorization data into the A10-P. The latter will search for the corresponding values
    according to the tag list and returns it as the transaction record.


===== PDF PAGE 133 =====

    Message flow:
                             HOST               Direction                 A10-P

                    Message T2B
                                                    →
                    (1st packet)
                                                            <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                           <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                            <EOT> if LRC error after 2 retries.
                                                           Message T2C (1st packet)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                    Message T2B
                    (2nd packet) with               →
                    [control] = ‘0’
                                                            <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                           <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                            <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           Message T2C (2nd packet)
                    …                                ...    …
                    Message T2B (final)             →
                                                            <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                           <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                            <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                            Message T2C
                                                    
                                                            (final packet) with [PktType] = ‘1’.
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message T2C                Response of Get Online authorization Data message with
                           multi packets
    Format:            <STX>T2C[PktType][SeqNo]<SUB>[record                       of       online   authorization
                       data]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)
    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             Return the online authorization data to the terminal.
    Message element:
                                        Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field          Length                Value and description

                   <STX>              1             <02>
                   T2C                3             Message ID
                   [PktType]          1             ‘0’ = There is still remaining data.
                                                    ‘1’ = The last packet.
                                                    '2' = sequence error.
                                                    '3' = Format error.
                   [SeqNo]            1             The packet sequence number, start from ‘0’.
                   <SUB>              1             <1A> as delimiter
                   [online            Var.          Hex string.
                   authorization                    Optional, if this transaction needs to be
                   data]                            authorized online.
                   <ETX>              1             <03>
                   [LRC]              1             Checksum
    Online authorization data:
    According to the tag list of online authorization data which is defined at tag# 4000000A, A10-P will
    search the corresponding values according to this tag list and returns a series of data objects with TLV
    (tag || length|| value) format as below:
    [EMV Tag Number (2 ~ 8 byte)] || [Length (2byte)] || [Value] <SUB>
    [EMV Tag Number (2 ~ 8 byte)] || [Length (2byte)] || [Value] <SUB>
    ........
    [EMV Tag Number (2 ~ 8 byte)] || [Length (2byte)] || [Value]
    When the A10-P responds to these data objects, it will convert their values from binary value to a hex
    decimal string if the data object format is “b” or “cn” or “n”.
    For example:
    The data of one record:
    9F02 || 06 || 000000001100 || 5A || 10 || 11223344556677889900AABBCCDDEEFF
    The terminal will see data:
    “9F0206000000001100<SUB>5A1011223344556677889900AABBCCDDEEFF”
    Message Flow:
        Refer to message T2B.

Message T2H                Get Transaction Data with Multiple Packets
    Format:           <STX>T2H[Request Type][control]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            This message is used to query the following types of transaction data:
                      - Online Authorization Data. (Equal to T27)
                      - Batch Data (Ref. EMV book4 12.1.5; Only for the latest transaction).
                      - Reversal Data. (Equal to T29)
                      If the output data objects are longer than the maximum packet length, A10-P will divide
                      them into multiple response packets

                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T2H               3             Message ID
                   [Request          1             ‘1’ = Online authorization data (object list
                   Type]                           defined by application config 4000000A)
                                                   ‘2’ = (Not supported)
                                                   ‘3’ = Batch data (application config
                                                   40000010)
                                                   ‘4’ = Reversal data (application config
                                                   4000000D)
                   [control]         1             Optional, as acknowledgment of multiple
                                                   packets
                                                   ‘0’ = ready for next packet
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum

                    packet)

                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                   →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                    Message T2H (2nd
                    packet) with [control] =          →
                    ‘0’
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)

Message T2I                Response of Transaction Data with Multiple Packets
    Format:              <STX>T2I[PktType][SeqNo]<SUB>[TLV records]<ETX>[LRC]
                         (response frame)
                         <STX>T2I[err code]<ETX>[LRC] (error frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:               This is the response message of message T2H.


    Message element:
                                        Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field          Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>              1             <02>
                   T2I                3             Message ID
                   [PktType]          1             ‘0’ = There is still remaining data.
                                                    ‘1’ = The last packet.
                   [SeqNo]            1             The packet sequence number, start from ‘0’.
                   <SUB>              1             <1A> as delimiter
                   [Condensed         Var.          The TLV (tag-length-value) records of the
                   TLV records]                     data objects listed in the application
                                                    configuration.
                   <ETX>              1             <03>
                   [LRC]              1             Checksum


    Details of Condensed TLV records:
    ▪ TLV record for a single data object is omitting field separator <FS>:
    [Tag Number (2 ~ 8 bytes hex)][Length (2bytes hex)][Value].
    ▪ Each data object is delimited by the <SUB> character, including the 1st one.
    ▪ The value with format codes ‘a’, ‘an’, ‘ans’ will be expressed as an ASCII string, and format codes ‘b’,
    ‘cn’, ‘n’, ‘var’ will be expressed as a hexadecimal string.


    Message element:
                                          Error frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field          Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>              1             <02>
                   T2I                3             Message ID
                                                    ‘2’ = Packet sequence error.
                   [ErrCode]          1
                                                    ‘3’ = Command format error.
                   <ETX>              1             <03>
                   [LRC]              1             Check value

Message T31                Extract Information on Smart Card (non-EMV
                           transactions)
    Format:            <STX>T31<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:             PIN Pad performs the application selection, initiate application processing and read
                       application data on the active smart card. All information will be stored in RAM
                       temporarily but it is not retrievable until host sends out the message T3C to force the
                       transaction declined or host sends out the message T35 to complete the transaction,
                       and then the host will be able to get the information by message T21.
                       PIN Pad will also prompt the user to insert a smart card if the card has not yet
                       presented. PIN Pad will send the message T12 (Application Selection Response) to
                       host.


    Message element:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length               Value and description

                    <STX>             1            <02>
                    T31               3            Message ID
                    <ETX>             1            <03>
                    [LRC]             1            Checksum


    Message flow:
                               HOST             Direction                   A10-P

                    Message T31                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              Application Select Response.
                                                    
                                                              Message T12
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message T34                Quick Chip Transaction Result
    Format:            <STX>T34[Status][Result]<SUB>[Control][Seq][ARQ_Data]<ETX>[LRC]
                       (response frame)
                       <STX>T34[Status][Reason][ErrCode]<ETX>[LRC] (error frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             The response of the Quick Chip transaction.


    Message element:
                                         Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field           Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>               1             <02>
                   T34                 3             Message ID
                                                     Optional, will be omitted for subsequent
                   [Status]            1
                                                     packets. ‘0’ for OK.
                                                     Optional, will be omitted for subsequent
                                                     packets. Transaction result codes:
                                                     “Y1”: Offline Approved,
                   [Result]            2
                                                     “Z1”: Offline Declined
                                                     “Y3”: Unable to go online, Offline Approved
                                                     “Z3”: Unable to go online, Offline Decline.
                   <SUB>               1             <1A> as delimiter
                                                     ‘0’: more data packets follow.
                   [Control]           1
                                                     ‘1’: the last data packet.
                   [Seq]               1             ‘0’ ~ ‘9’: data sequence number.
                                                     Pre-defined EMV data objects output for
                   [ARQ_Data]          Var.          online authorization. Data object list defined
                                                     in private tag 4000000A (see message T27.)
                   <ETX>               1             <03>
                   [LRC]               1             Check value


    Detail of ARQ_Data (Condensed TLV records):
    ▪ TLV record for a single data object is omitting field separator <FS>:
    [Tag Number (2 ~ 8 bytes hex)][Length (2bytes hex)][Value].
    ▪ Each data object is delimited by the <SUB> character, including the 1st one.
    ▪ The value with format codes ‘a’, ‘an’, ‘ans’ will be expressed as an ASCII string, and format codes ‘b’,
    ‘cn’, ‘n’, ‘var’ will be expressed as a hexadecimal string.


===== PDF PAGE 142 =====

    Message element:
                                           Error frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field           Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>               1             <02>
                   T34                 3             Message ID
                   [Status]            1             ‘1’ for Fail.
                                                     ‘1’: Fatal Error
                                                     ‘2’: T33 Command Format Error
                   [Reason]            1
                                                     ‘3’: Transaction Canceled or timed out.
                                                     ‘4’: ICC error, fallback to MSR processing.
                   [ErrCode]           8             (Optional, if reason = ‘1’), overall error code.
                   <ETX>               1             <03>
                   [LRC]               1             Check value


    Message Flow:
        Refer to message T33.


    Example:
    If an ARQ data needs two T34 packets to transmit, the first packet format will be:
    <STX>T340Z3<SUB>01[Tag1][Len1][Value1]<SUB>[Tag2]……<ETX>[LRC]
    The second and (the last) packet format will be:
    <STX>T34<SUB>12[TagN][LenN][ValueN]<SUB>[TagN+1]……<ETX>[LRC]

Message T35                Start Transaction (non-EMV transactions)
    Format:           <STX>T35<SUB>[AmtAuth]<SUB>[AmtOther]<SUB>[CurExponent][CurCode]
                      <SUB>[TranType]<SUB>[TranInfo]<SUB>[Account                            Type]<SUB>[Force
                      Online] <SUB>[Encrypted Session key]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            After receiving this message command T35, PIN Pad will perform a complete
                      non-EMV transaction but processed in the same order as EMV transaction flow (the
                      flow will cover ‘Data Authentication’ through ‘Completion’, see EMV 4.3, Book 3, chap
                      8.2, figure 6 - transaction flow example) based on the selected EMV application that
                      has corrected application name on the T12 (Application Select response). PIN pad will
                      also prompt the user to do the appropriated entry when presented, like confirm or enter
                      PIN code. PIN pad will send the message T16 (Start Transaction Response) to the
                      host.
                      If the IC card can’t be read (which has known from T12 response code with “T1214”
                      when in message T31 processing and terminal changed to do magnetic stripe card
                      processing; please refer the Notes in message T12), then after receiving this message
                      T35, PIN pad will not process the EMV transaction flow. But in place of just storing the
                      information provided from this message and returns the message T16 with result code
                      “A1” to ask the terminal to go online to get the authorization, then send back a
                      message T17 to finish this transaction. Terminal could have extra operation on
                      magnetic stripe card transaction (like issue message Q1 to read track data and issue
                      message 70 for PIN entry, and so on.) before issuing this message T35.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T35               3             Message ID
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [AmtAuth]         12            Hexadecimal, Amount Authorized tag
                                                   ‘0x9F02’
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [AmtOther]        12            Hexadecimal, Amount Other, tag ‘0x9F03’
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [CurExponent]     1             Hexadecimal, tag ‘0x5F36’
                   [CurCode]         3             Hexadecimal, tag ‘0x5F2A’.
                                                   For example, USD$ = 0x840
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>


===== PDF PAGE 144 =====

                    [TranType]        2            Hexadecimal, Transaction Type, tag ‘0x9c’
                    <SUB>             1            <1A>
                    [TranInfo]        2            Transaction Info, tag ‘0x60000001’
                    <SUB>             1            <1A>
                    [Account          2            Account Type, tag ‘0x5F57’
                    Type]
                    <SUB>             1            <1A>
                    [Force Online]    1            ‘1’: Force this transaction to be authorized
                                                   online.   Valid only if the terminal
                                                   configuration enables the capability to support
                                                   online authorization.
                                                    ‘0’: Run the default EMV authorization flow
                    <SUB>             1            <1A> (Optional, if the Encrypted Session key
                                                   exists)
                    [Encrypted        16 or 32     (Optional, if the transaction needs the user to
                    Session key]      or 48        enter the PIN for online authorization. If no
                                                   session key exists, the A10-P will use PIN
                                                   encryption key(Usage P0) or DUKPT key
                                                   exist in A10-P). Only TDES session key are
                                                   used for EMV online PIN entry.
                    <ETX>             1            <03>
                    [LRC]             1            Checksum


    Message flow:

                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)

                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message T37                EMV PIN Management
    Format:            <STX>T37<SUB>[Operation]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             After receive this message command T37, PIN Pad will perform the corresponding
                       operation for PIN Change, PIN Unblock, PIN Verification process.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length                Value and description

                    <STX>            1             <02>
                    T37              3             Message ID
                    <SUB>            1             <1A>
                    [Operation]      1             ‘1’: Change PIN.
                                                   ‘2’: Unblock PIN.
                                                   ‘3’: Verify PIN.
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                              HOST              Direction                   A10-P

                    Message T37                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              (<EOT> after 3 NAKs)
                                                              EMV PIN Management Response.
                                                    
                                                              Message T38
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    (<EOT> after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 146 =====

Message T38                EMV PIN Management Response
    Format:           <STX>T38[Status][Reason][Err Message][Result]<ETX>[LRC]
                      (response frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            The message contains the transaction result on the smart card to be sent to terminal.


    Message element:
                                        Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                       Field            Length                Value and description

                    <STX>               1            <02>
                    T38                 3            Message ID
                    Status              1            0:OK; 1:Fail
                    Reason              1            Optional. (If Status = 1)
                                                     1:Fatal Error
                                                     2:Command Format Error
                                                     3:Transaction Canceled
                    [Err                8            Optional, if Reason = ‘1’Hex String
                    Message]
                    [Result]            2            Optional. (If Status = 0)
                                                     ‘A1’: Online Authorize Request,
                                                     ‘A4’: Application reselection.
                                                     ‘V0’: PIN verification succeed.
                                                     ‘V1’: PIN verification failed.
                    <ETX>               1            <03>
                    [LRC]               1            Checksum


    Message flow:     Please refer to message T37.

Message T3C                Force to Complete the Transaction (non-EMV transactions)
    Format:           <STX>T3C<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:            The message used to complete the non-EMV transaction.


    Message element:

                          Field        Length               Value and description

                    <ETX>            1             <03>

                    Message T3C                     →

                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              Respond results as Message T16.
                                                             The Message ID (T16) is replaced
                                                              by T3C.

===== PDF PAGE 152 =====

Section 7             Contactless EMV Level 2 Transaction Messages
Contactless EMV Level2 transaction messages are divided into 2 groups. One group are the
EMV-configuration data operation messages (T51, T53, T55, T75, T77) and the other group are the
EMV-transaction messages (T61, T63, T65, T6C, T71 and T73).


The issuing sequence of the PCD EMV transaction messages is controlled by the A10-P. An invalid
sequence will terminate that PCD EMV transaction. Message T61 is used for the terminal-side to transmit
transaction information such as the amount to be used by the A10-P to complete the transaction with the card
if the transaction does not need to be authorized online. Message T71 is applied if the transaction needs to
be authorized online. The terminal-side will transmit necessary information via this message to the A10-P to
complete the rest of transactional steps. If the response from the host contains issuer script (see EMV Book),
the terminal-side will issue message T73 to send these scripts into the A10-P. Then the A10-P will issue
these scripts at appropriate time to the card. Message T6C is used to terminate an EMV transaction. Finally,
message T65 is used for terminal-side to get the transaction information through out the PCD EMV
transaction. The transaction flow can be referred in the “Overall Contactless EMV level 2 transaction flow
reference” section.


===== PDF PAGE 153 =====

The meanings of error codes in the [Err Message] are listed below:


    Error Code                                             Error Description
00000003            Service not accepted.
00000F9B            Store configuration data error.
8FFF0001            Out of memory.
8FFF0002            Parameter error.
8FFFFF02            Tag’s data format error.
8FFFFF03            Some mandatory tags are not configured well.
8FFFFFF0            Command format error.
8FFFFFF1            The sequence of EMV transaction command is erroneous.
8FFFFFF2            Terminal fundamental data is missing.
8FFFFFF3            Authentication failed.
8FFFFF1A            Authentication key expired.
8FFFFF1B            Terminal configuration data is missing.
8FFFFF1C            No configuration data of EMV application or the data is missing.
8FFFFFF4            The storage space of batch data capture is full.
8FFFFFF5            No terminal configuration data, EMV application configuration data or CA public key.
A2000001            Application initial conditions are not satisfied.
A2000002            The generated cryptogram is not allowed.
A2000007            The specified instruction ID    is not recognized.
A200000A            The type    of the requested application cryptogram is incorrect.
A200000C            The status response code (SW1 SW2) is          not ‘9000’.
A200000D            The Generate AC command is called more than 2 times in the current transaction.
A200000E            The AIP mandatory data is missing in the card response data .
A200000F            The AFL mandatory data is missing in the card response data .
A2000010            The response template from the card is incorrect.
A2000011            The format of AFL is incorrect.
A2000013            A redundant data output from the card is not allowed.
A2000014            Missing mandatory data in the card response data .
EFFFFFFF            EMV transaction cancelled.
DFFFFFFF            EMV forced abort.
FFFF0001            There is an error when parsing the TLV list data.
FFFFFFFF            The contactless card does not respond data.


===== PDF PAGE 154 =====

Message T51                PCD Terminal Configuration Setup
    Format:             <STX>T51[Pkt No.][Total Pkts]<SUB>[DO]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:              Host can use this command to send terminal configuration data to the A10-P. This
                        command can be sent many times. A10-P will save those data internally and apply
                        them during the transaction. A10-P will send the message T52 (Terminal Configuration
                        Setup Response) to the host as the response. The functions of Message T51 and
                        Message T01 are exactly the same and can be used alternately.


    Message element:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                           Field        Length                 Value and description

                     <STX>            1             <02>
                     T51              3             Message ID
                     [Pkt No.]        1             Decimal. Packet sequence number (1 ~ 9)(ex.
                                                    2)
                     [Total Pkts]     1             Decimal. Total packets (1 ~ 9)(ex. 8).
                     <SUB>            1             <1A>
                     [DO]             Var.          Data Object, format as below
                     <ETX>            1             <03>
                     [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Data Object Format: each data object is delimited by <SUB>, and each field inside each data
    object is delimited by <FS>.


    Data Format: (Please also refer to the EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4,)
  Format                                                    Description
     1       a - Alphabetic data (a ~z, A~Z)
     2       b - unsigned binary numbers or bit combinations
     3       an - Alphanumeric data (a ~z, A~Z, 0~9)
     4       ans - Alphanumeric Special data (Characters defined in ISO8859)
     5       cn - Compressed numeric data (0~9, left justify, pad hexadecimal ‘F’s. Ex. 12 34 56 12 3F FF)
     6       n - Numeric data (0~9, right justify, pad leading hexadecimal zeroes. Ex. 00 00 00 01 23 45)
     7       var - Variable data (Any bit combination)


===== PDF PAGE 155 =====

   Note. User has to follow the restriction specified in the EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4, and
           Appendix E of this document to load the configuration data. A10-P will check if the length
           of each configuration data item is consistent. Any inconsistent data item will make the data
           loading fail.
    Note. If the data format is ‘2’ (binary), ‘5’(compressed numeric), ‘6’(numeric) or ‘7’(variable data),
           it cannot be allocated in message T51 directly. It should be transferred into a hexadecimal
           string and then allocated in message T51.


    Example: (Clear the terminal configuration data and then setup the new data.)
    Merchant Category Code: 0000 (Numerical)
    Terminal ID:                 SmartPOS (Ascii)


    <STX>T5111<SUB>9f15<FS>6<FS>0000<SUB>9f1c<FS>3<FS>SmartPOS<ETX>[LRC]


    A10-P will check if the terminal downloads the minimum set of terminal-related information into itself. The
    download process will fail if there is not enough data in this message. Please refer to Appendix F for the
    minimum set of terminal-related data.


===== PDF PAGE 156 =====

    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction                A10-P

                    1st Message T51                →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                          1st Message T52
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                    2nd Message T51                →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                          2nd Message T52
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                    …                              …       …
                    Last one Message T51           →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                          Last one Message T52
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 157 =====

Message T52                PCD Terminal Configuration Setup Response
    Format:            <STX>T52[Result][Reason][Err Msg]<ETX>[LRC]
                       (response frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             The response message of message T51. The functions of Message T52 and Message
                       T02 are exactly the same and can be used alternately.


    Message element:
                                          Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field           Length                  Value and description

                    <STX>               1             <02>
                    T52                 3             Message ID
                    [Result]            1             ‘0’: OK,
                                                      ‘1’: Fail
                    [Reason]            1             <Optional, if Res = ‘1’>
                                                      ‘1’: Fatal Error
                                                      ‘2’: Format Error
                    [Err Msg]           8             Optional, if Reason = ‘1’, Hex decimal string.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum


    Message flow:
        Refer to message T51.

Message T53                PCD Certification Authority Public Key Setup
    Usage:             In A10-P, Message T53 is as same as Message T03. They have the same format, but
                      only Message ID is different. In other words, PCD CAPK and ICC CAPK are the same.
                      Using Message T53 to setup CAPK will have the same result by using Message T03.
                      For the detailed format and usage, please refer to Section 6 Message T03.

                       T04 are exactly the same and can be used alternately.

                    [Sequence]          1             Same sequence number of the corresponded
                                                      T53 message.
                    [Result]            1             ‘0’: OK,
                                                      ‘1’: Fail
                    [Reason]            1             ‘1’: Fatal Error
                                                      ‘2’: Format Error
                                                      ‘3’: Authentication Fail

Message T55                PCD Application Configuration Setup
    Format:           <STX>T55[Pkt No.][TotalPkts]<SUB>[TXN]<SUB>[KID]<SUB>[AID]<SUB>
                      [DO]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)
                      <STX>T55[Pkt No.][Total Pkts]<SUB>[DO]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)
    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            Host can use this command to send out the EMV application configuration data to the
                      A10-P. This command can be sent many times but each command is only used for one
                      application. A10-P will save those data inside and use them during the transaction.
                      A10-P will send message T56 (EMV Application Configuration Setup Response) to the
                      host. The data installed into the A10-P via this message will be saved       in the internal
                      storage structure with a name same as in the [AID] field.


    Message element:
    1st Message:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T55               3             Message ID
                   [Pkt No.]         1             Decimal. Packet sequence number (1)
                   [Total Pkts]      1             Decimal. Total packets (1 ~ 9)(ex. 8).
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [TXN]             2             Transaction type, refer to EMV Contactless
                                                   Specifications v2.4.
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [KID]             6             Kernel ID, refer to Appendix E.
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [AID]             10~32         EMV Application ID, refer to EMV
                                                   Contactless Specifications v2.4.
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [DO]              Var.          Data Object, format as below
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 161 =====

    Rest of Message (If there are 2 more messages):
                                            Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                            Field        Length                 Value and description

                      <STX>             1            <02>
                      T55               3            Message ID
                      [Pkt No.]         1            Decimal. Packet sequence number (2 ~ 9) (ex.
                                                     2).
                      [Total Pkts]      1            Decimal. Total packets (2 ~ 9)(ex. 8).
                      <SUB>             1            <1A>
                      [DO]              Var.         Data Object, format as below
                      <ETX>             1            <03>
                      [LRC]             1            Checksum


    Format of Data Objects:
    Each data object shall include three data fields delimited by <FS>: [Tag#]<FS>[Format
    Code]<FS>[Value]. Each data object is delimited by a <SUB> to construct multiple <DO>s. The [tag#] is
    defined in the EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4. There are also some private tags defined in
    Appendix E.


    Data Format: (Please also refer to EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4)
    Format                                                   Description
       1        a - Alphabetic data (a ~z, A~Z)
       2        b - unsigned binary numbers or bit combinations
       3        an - Alphanumeric data (a ~z, A~Z, 0~9)
       4        ans - Alphanumeric Special data (Characters defined in ISO8859)
       5        cn - Compressed numeric data (0~9, left justify, pad hexadecimal ‘F’s. Ex. 12 34 56 12 3F FF)
       6        n - Numeric data (0~9, right justify, pad leading hexadecimal zeroes. Ex. 00 00 00 01 23 45)
       7        var - Variable data (Any bit combination)


   Note. User has to follow the restriction specified in EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4 and
           Appendix E of this document to load the configuration data. A10-P will check if the length of
           each configuration data item is consistent. Any inconsistent data item will make the data
           loading fail.
    Note. If the data format is ‘2’ (binary), ‘5’(compressed numeric), ‘6’(numeric) or ‘7’(variable data),
           it    cannot be allocated in message T55 directly. It should be transferred into a hexadecimal
           string and then allocated in message T55.


===== PDF PAGE 162 =====

    Example:
    Zero Amount Allowed:         01 (binary)
    Contactless Floor Limit:     00 00 00 00 80 00 (numerical)
    VLP Support Indicator:       01 (VLP supported) (binary)


    <STX>T5511<SUB>00<SUB>030000<SUB>A00000031010<SUB>40000042<FS>2<FS>01
    <SUB>40000043<FS>6<FS>000000008000<SUB>9F7A<FS>2<FS>01<ETX>[LRC]


    A10-P saves these data and give an ID as “A00000031010” to this group of data.
    The A10-P will check if the terminal downloads the minimum set of EMV Application-related information
    into itself. The downloading process will fail if there is not enough data in this message. Please refer to
    Appendix F for the minimum set of EMV Application-related data.


    Message flow:
                             HOST              Direction                A10-P

                    1st Message T55                →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                          1st Message T56
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                    2nd Message T55                →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           Application Select Response.
                                                   
                                                           2nd Message T56
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                    …                              …       …
                    Last one Message T55           →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           Application Select Response.
                                                   
                                                           Last one Message T56


===== PDF PAGE 163 =====

    Message flow:

    Format:           <STX>T59[Config Type][control]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)

    Message element:

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T59               3             Message ID

                                                   2: All the IDs of the PCD application data of
                                                   Purchase.
                   [control]         1             (option)'0': Ready for next packet.
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum

                            HOST               Direction               A10-P

                    Message T59                    →

                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message T5A                Response of PCD Config Data Query Message
    Format:            <STX>T5A[Config Type][Result]<SUB>[ID List]<ETX>[LRC]
                       (response frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             The response message of message T59.


    Message element:
                                          Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field           Length                  Value and description

                    <STX>               1             <02>
                    T5A                 3             Message ID
                    [Config Type]       1             0: Unknow type.
                                                      1: All the IDs of PCD public key.
                                                      2: All the IDs of PCD application data of
                                                      Purchase.
                                                      3: All the IDs of the PCD application data of
                                                      Cash.
                                                      4: All the IDs of the PCD application data of
                                                      Cashback.
                                                      5: All the IDs of the PCD application data of
                                                      Refund.
                    [Result]            1             0: There is data in identified data type.
                                                      1: No data in identified data type.
                                                      2: Wrong configuration type.
                                                      3: Arrangement exceeded.
                                                      4: Fatal error.
                    <SUB>               1             <1A>, Optional if Result is 0, 3.
                    [ID List]           Var.          Optional, if Result is 0, 3.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum


    [ID List]: The concatenation of IDs. There is a <FS> between each ID.
    Ex. [ID List] = A00000000390<FS> A000000004F8<FS> A000000004F5 (Config Type = 1)
        [ID List] = A0000000041010<FS>B0123456781234<FS> A000000010 (Config Type = 2)


    Message flow:
        Refer to message T59.

Message T5B                Delete PCD Configuration Data Message.
    Format:            <STX>T5B[Config Type]<SUB>[ID List]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             Host can use this command to delete the PCD application configuration data or the CA
                       public key in the A10-P. This command can be sent many times.


    Message element:
                                           Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length                Value and description

                    <STX>              1           <02>
                    T5B                3           Message ID
                    [Config Type]      1           1: CA public key.
                                                   2: EMV application data.
                    <SUB>              1           <1A>
                    [ID List]          Var         List of IDs that will be erased.
                    <ETX>              1           <03>
                    [LRC]              1           Checksum


    [ID List]: The concatenation of IDs. There is a <FS> between each ID.
    Ex. [ID List] = A00000000390<FS> A000000004F8<FS> A000000004F5 (Config Type = 1)
        [ID List] = A0000000041010<FS>B0123456781234<FS> A000000010 (Config Type = 2)


    Message flow:
                                HOST             Direction                    A10-P

                    Message T5B                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                             Message T5C
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 168 =====

Message T5C                Response of Delete PCD Configuration Data Message.
    Format:            <STX>T5C[Config Type][Status]<SUB>[Del Result]<ETX>[LRC]
                       (response frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             The response message of message T5B.


    Message element:
                                          Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field           Length                 Value and description

                    <STX>               1             <02>
                    T5C                 3             Message ID
                    [Config Type]       1             0: Unknow type.
                                                      1: CA public key.
                                                      2: EMV application data.
                    [Status]            1             0: Delete OK
                                                      1: Fatal error.
                                                      2: Format error.
                                                      4. Sequence error.
                    <SUB>               1             <1A>, Optional, if Status is 0.
                    [Del Result]        var            The results of the delete operation on the
                                                      indicated ID
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum


    [Del Result]:
    It’s the concatenation of the results for the delete operation on the indicated ID. There is a <FS> between
    each result. If the delete operation is successful (when A10-P finds out the matched one and delete it
    successfully), the result is 0. If there is no such ID as the terminal specified, the result is 1.


    Ex. Terminal issues message T5B to erase the EMV application data with ID “A0000000102020”,
    “A0000020” and “A0000000103030”. Now the A10-P keeps the EMV application data with ID
    “A0000000103030” only.
    The data in the [Del Result] field will be “1<FS>1<FS>0”.


    Message flow:
        Refer to message T5B.

                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)

                                                             Message T5E10
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message T5F                PCD DRL Configuration Setup.
    Format:           <STX>T5F<SUB>[AID]<SUB>[DO]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            Host can use this command to send DRL(Dynamic Reader Limit) configuration data to
                      the A10-P. This command can be sent many times. A10-P will save those data
                      internally and apply them during the transaction. A10-P will send the message T5G
                      (PCD DRL Configuration Setup Response) to the host as the response.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T5F               3             Message ID
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   AID               2~32          EMV Application ID, refer to EMV
                                                   Contactless Specifications v2.4, Hex decimal
                                                   string.
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [DO]              Var.          Data Object, format as below
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


    Format of Data Objects:
    Each data object shall include three data fields delimited by <FS>: [Tag#]<FS>[Format Code]<FS>
    [Value] Each data object is delimited by a <SUB> to construct multiple <DO>s. The [tag#] defined in
    EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4, section 4.3.


===== PDF PAGE 172 =====

    Data Format: (Please also refer to EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4)
  Format                                                   Description
     1      a - Alphabetic data (a ~z, A~Z)
     2      b - unsigned binary numbers or bit combinations
     3      an - Alphanumeric data    (a ~z, A~Z, 0~9)
     4      ans - Alphanumeric Special data (Characters defined in ISO8859)
     5      cn - Compressed numeric data (0~9, left justify, pad hexadecimal ‘F’s. Ex. 12 34 56 12 3F FF)
     6      n - Numeric data (0~9, right justify, pad leading hexadecimal zeroes. Ex. 00 00 00 01 23 45)
     7      var - Variable data (Any bit combination)


    Message flow:
                             HOST               Direction                   A10-P

                    Message T5F                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                             Message T5G
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 173 =====
