===== PDF PAGE 176 =====

Message T61                Start Transaction
    Format:           <STX>T61<SUB>[AmtAuth]<SUB>[AmtOther]<SUB>
                      [CurExponent][CurCode]<SUB>[TranType]<SUB>[TranInfo]<SUB>
                      [Account Type]<SUB>[Force Online]<SUB>
                      [Encrypted Session key]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            After receiving message T61, the A10-P will perform a complete EMV transaction
                      (the flow will cover from ‘Initiate Application’ through ‘Completion’, see EMV
                      Contactless Specifications 2.4, book A, chap 5.3, figure 5-2 - Logical Architecture).
                      A10-P will send message T62 (Start Transaction Response) to the host as the
                      response.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T61               3             Message ID
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [AmtAuth]         12            Optional: Numerical string (include digits
                                                   after decimal point) to describe the “Amount
                                                   Authrozied” used in the purchase transaction.
                                                   Map to EMV tag ‘0x9f02’
                                                   Ex: “000000012345” means 123.45 if
                                                   [CurExponent] is set to ‘2’.
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [AmtOther]        12            Optional: Numerical string (include digits
                                                   after decimal point) to describe the “Amount,
                                                   Other” used in the special transaction, such as
                                                   the “cashback” amount. Map to EMV tag
                                                   ‘0x9f03’
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [CurExponent]     1             Numerical digit (‘0’ to ‘3’) to describe the
                                                   “Transaction Currency Exponent”, which is
                                                   the count of digits after the decimal point.
                                                   Map to EMV tag ‘0x5f36’
                   [CurCode]         3             Numerical string to describe the “Transaction
                                                   Currency Code”. Map to EMV tag ‘0x5f2a’.
                                                   Ex: USD = “840”


===== PDF PAGE 177 =====

                   <SUB>            1             <1A>
                   [TranType]       2             Numerical string to indicate the type of
                                                  transaction, presented by the first two digits of
                                                  the ISO 8583:1987 field 3 (processing code.)
                                                  Map to EMV tag ‘0x9c’
                                                  Ex.
                                                  “00” – purchase,
                                                  “01” – cash advance,
                                                   “09” – purchase with cashback,
                                                   “20” – refund.
                                                  The actual values to be used are defined by
                                                  the relevant payment system.
                   <SUB>            1             <1A>
                   [TranInfo]       2             Hexadecimal string to indicate the private
                                                  parameter “Transaction Info”. Map to private
                                                  tag ‘0x60000001’
                                                  Allowable values:
                                                   “80” - Cash (cash advance)
                                                   “40” - Goods
                                                   “20” - Services
                                                  “10” - Cashback
                   <SUB>            1             <1A>
                   [Account         2             Account Type, Map to EMV tag ‘0x5f57’
                   Type]                          Allowable values (ref. EMV v4.3 book3
                                                  Annex G)
                                                  “00” – Default
                                                  “10” – Savings
                                                  “20” – Cheque / Debit
                                                  “30” – Credit.
                   <SUB>            1             <1A>
                   [Force Online]   1             ‘1’: Force this transaction to be authorized
                                                  online.   Valid only if the terminal
                                                  configuration enables the capability to support
                                                  online authorization.
                                                  ‘0’: Run the default EMV authorization flow
                   <SUB>            1             <1A>
                   [Encrypted       16 or 32      Optional, TDES session keythat is used to
                   Session key]     or 48         encrypt the PIN entry when CVM asks for
                                                  online PIN verification.


===== PDF PAGE 178 =====

                                                   If the CVM asks for online PIN verification
                                                   but this session key is not entered, A10-P will
                                                   ignore the PIN entry request and indicate that
                                                   no PIN is entered in the TVR register.
                    <SUB>            1             <1A>,Optional, only if [DO] exists
                    DO               Var.          Data Object, format as below
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Format of Data Objects:
    Each data object shall include three data fields delimited by <FS>: [Tag#]<FS>[Format
    Code]<FS>[Value]. Each data object is delimited by a <SUB> to construct multiple <DO>s. The [tag#] is
    defined in the EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4. There are also some private tags defined in
    Appendix E.


    Data Format: (Please also refer to EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4)
  Format                                                   Description
     1      a - Alphabetic data (a ~z, A~Z)
     2      b - unsigned binary numbers or bit combinations
     3      an - Alphanumeric data (a ~z, A~Z, 0~9)
     4      ans - Alphanumeric Special data (Characters defined in ISO8859)
     5      cn - Compressed numeric data (0~9, left justify, pad hexadecimal ‘F’s. Ex. 12 34 56 12 3F FF)
     6      n - Numeric data (0~9, right justify, pad leading hexadecimal zeroes. Ex. 00 00 00 01 23 45)
     7      var - Variable data (Any bit combination)


   Note. User has to follow the restriction specified in EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4 and
           Appendix E of this document to load the configuration data. A10-P will check if the length of
           each configuration data item is consistent. Any inconsistent data item will make the data
           loading fail.
    Note. If the data format is ‘2’ (binary), ‘5’(compressed numeric), ‘6’(numeric) or ‘7’(variable data),
           it cannot be allocated in message T61 directly. It should be transferred into a hexadecimal
           string and then allocated in message T61.


===== PDF PAGE 179 =====

    Example:
    Balance Read Before Gen AC:           000000 (Numeric)
    Balance Read After Gen AC:            000000 (Numeric)
    [DO]:
    DF8104<FS>6<FS>000000<SUB>DF8105<FS>6<FS>000000


    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction                 A10-P

                    Message T61                    →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           Start Transaction Response.
                                                   
                                                           Message T62


===== PDF PAGE 180 =====

Message T62                Start Transaction Response
    Format:            <STX>T62[Status][Reason][Err Message][Result]<ETX>[LRC]
                       (response frame)
    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             The message contains the transaction result on the smart card to be sent to the
                       terminal.
    Message element:
                                          Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field           Length                  Value and description

                    <STX>               1             <02>
                    T62                 3             Message ID
                    [Status]            1             0:OK; 1:Fail
                    [Reason]            1             Optional. (If Status = 1)
                                                      1:Fatal Error
                                                      2:Command Format Error
                    [Err Message]       8             Optional, if Reason = ‘1’Hex String
                                                      ‘00000007’: Please see note.
                    [Result]            2             Optional. (If Status = 0)
                                                      ‘Y1’: Offline Approved,
                                                      ‘Z1’: Offline Declined
                                                      ‘Y3’: Unable to go online,
                                                              Offline Approved
                                                      ‘Z3’: Unable to go online, Offline Declined.
                                                      ‘Y4’: Online Approved
                                                      ‘Z4’: Online Declined
                                                      ‘Y7’: Offline Approved With Signature
                                                      ‘Y8’: Unable to go online,
                                                              Offline Approved With Signature
                                                      ‘Y9’: Online Approved With Signature
                                                      ‘A1’: Online Authorize Request
                                                      ‘B0’: Try another interface.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum
    Note: If the [Result] is ‘A1’, theterminal should send the online authorization request to the issuer host
           and After      that, the terminal shall send message T71 to the A10-P to continue the transaction.
    Note: For special error code, if the [Status] is ‘1’, [Reason] is ‘1’, [Err Message] is ‘00000007’, the
           terminal shall send message T61 to Start Transaction.
    Message flow:
        Refer to message T61.


===== PDF PAGE 181 =====

    ........

    necessary data from the A10-P for online authorization. And the A10-P will wait for the terminal to send
    message T71 to        inform the A10-P on the go online authorization result


    A10-P will search for the corresponding values according to this tag list and return message T66 after
    receiving message T65 from the terminal. Please refer to Appendix F for more details.

                   data]

===== PDF PAGE 187 =====

    Online authorization data:
    A10-P returns a series of data objects with the TLV (tag || length|| value) format as below:


    [EMV Tag Number (2 ~ 8 byte)] || [Length (2byte)] || [Value]
    [EMV Tag Number (2 ~ 8 byte)] || [Length (2byte)] || [Value]
    ........
    [EMV Tag Number (2 ~ 8 byte)] || [Length (2byte)] || [Value].
    When the A10-P responds these data objects, it will convert       them from binary values to a hex decimal
    string if the data object format is “b” or “cn” or “n”. For example, the data of one record is 0x9F02 || 06 ||
    000000001100 || 5A || 10 || 11223344556677889900AABBCCDDEEFF, the terminal will see
    “9F02060000000011005A1011223344556677889900AABBCCDDEEFF”.


    Message flow:
        Refer to message T65

Message T67                Get Transaction Payment Scheme
    Format:            <STX>T67<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:             A10-P      to retrieve the payment scheme after the EMV transaction is done. The A10-P
                       will send message T68 (Get Transaction Payment Scheme Response) to the host as a
                       response.


    Message element:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length               Value and description

                    <STX>             1            <02>
                    T67               3            Message ID
                    <ETX>             1            <03>
                    [LRC]             1            Checksum


    Message flow:
                              HOST              Direction                   A10-P

                    Message T67                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                             Message T68
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message T68                Get Transaction Payment Scheme Response
    Format:           <STX>T68[Scheme]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Variable 7 to 8 bytes.
    Usage:            The message contains the transaction payment scheme to be sent to the host.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>

                   <ETX>             1             <03>

                      (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            After receiving this message T71, the A10-P will continue to perform the EMV
                      transaction if the previous T62 response result is ‘A1’ (online authorized request, see
                      EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4). A10-P will respond message T62 (Start
                      Transaction Response) to this T71 to the host.

                   [Online Res]      1             ‘0’: Unable to go online
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum

                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>

                   [Online Res]      1             ‘1’: Get Online Authorize Response,
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>

                   <SUB>             1             <1A>

                                                   string.
                                                   If there is an IAD response from the remote
                                                   host , the terminal shall send this to the
                                                   A10-P.
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum

Message T73                 Send Issuer Script Command
    Format:               <STX>T73[IS]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:                A10-P performs the Issuer script processing as in the EMV transaction flow after
                          receiving this command from the host as the response message after performing
                          online authorization. This command can be sent many times if too many script
                          commands need to be processed. But the last one should be sent before message
                          T71 .


    Message element:
                                                 Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                            Field              Length                 Value and description

                     <STX>                   1              <02>
                     T73                     3              Message ID
                     IS                      Var.           Issuer Script, format as follows. Hex string.
                     <ETX>                   1              <03>
                     [LRC]                   1              Checksum


    Issuer Script Format: (see EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4)
                T                 L                 T          L             Script ID              Commands
          ‘71’ or ‘72’      Including            ‘9F18’      ‘04’      Identifier (4 bytes)    Issuer Script Command
                          Script ID, tags,                                                               Format
                              lengths                                                               (see below)


    Issuer Script Command Format: (see EMV Contactless Specifications v2.4)
         T1         L1        V1        T2           L2      V2       T3        L3        V3      Tx         Lx      Vx
         ‘86’   L(V1)        Cmd        ‘86’        L(V2)   Cmd       ‘86’    L(V3)      Cmd      ‘86’     L(Vx)     cmd


    Message flow:
                                  HOST                    Direction                      A10-P

                         Message T73                         →
                                                                         <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                                        <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                         <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                        Message T74
                         <ACK> (Good LRC)
                         <NAK> (Bad LRC)                      →
                         <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message T75                 Revocation List Setup
    Format:             <STX>T75<SUB>[RID][SN][PKI]<ETX>[LRC]                    (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 25 bytes
    Usage:              Host can use this command to send the revocation key information to the A10-P. This
                        command can be sent many times. A10-P will save this information internally and
                        check that during the transaction. The A10-P will send message T76 (Revocation List
                        Setup Response) to the host as the response.


    Message elements:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                           Field        Length                  Value and description

                     <STX>            1             <02>
                     T75              3             Message ID
                     <SUB>            1             Separator
                     [RID]            10            The RID for the revocated public key. Present
                                                    in hexstring.
                     [SN]             6             The serial number of the revocated public key.
                                                    Present in hexstring.
                     [PKI]            2             The public key index of the revocated key.
                                                    Present in hexstring.
                     <ETX>            1             <03>
                     [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Example:
    Revocation list information
    RID:        A0 00 00 00 03
    SN:         00 00 01
    PKI:        51


    <STX>T75<SUB> A00000000300000151<ETX>[LRC]

                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

                    No.]                              data.

Message T81                Start EMV Contact/Contactless Transaction
    Format:           <STX>T81<SUB>[AmtAuth]<SUB>[AmtOther]<SUB>[CurExponent][CurCode]
                      <SUB>[TranType]<SUB>[TranInfo]<SUB>[Account                                 Type]<SUB>[Force
                      Online]<SUB>[Encrypted Session key]<SUB>[DO]<ETX>[LRC]
                      (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            After receiving this message T81, Reader will perform a complete EMV transaction
                      (the flow will cover from ‘Initiate Application’ through ‘Completion’, see EMV
                      Contactless Specifications v2.4, book A, chap 5.3, figure 5-2 - Logical Architecture, or
                      see EMV 4.3, book 3, chap 8.2, figure 6 - transaction flow example). Reader will send
                      message T12/T16/T62 (Contact/Contactless Transaction Response) to the host as a
                      response.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   T81               3             Message ID
                   [AmtAuth]         12            Optional: Numerical string (include digits
                                                   after decimal point) to describe the “Amount
                                                   Authrozied” used in the purchase transaction.
                                                   Map to EMV tag ‘0x9f02’
                                                   Ex: “000000012345” means 123.45 if
                                                   [CurExponent] is set to ‘2’.
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [AmtOther]        12            Optional: Numerical string (include digits
                                                   after decimal point) to describe the “Amount,
                                                   Other” used in the special transaction, such as
                                                   the “cashback” amount. Map to EMV tag
                                                   ‘0x9f03’
                   <SUB>             1             <1A>
                   [CurExponent]     1             Numerical digit (‘0’ to ‘3’) to describe the
                                                   “Transaction Currency Exponent”, which is
                                                   the count of digits after the decimal point.
                                                   Map to EMV tag ‘0x5f36’
                   [CurCode]         3             Numerical string to describe the “Transaction
                                                   Currency Code”. Map to EMV tag ‘0x5f2a’.
                                                   Ex: USD = “840”


===== PDF PAGE 202 =====

                   <SUB>            1             <1A>
                   [TranType]       2             Numerical string to indicate the type of
                                                  transaction, presented by the first two digits of
                                                  the ISO 8583:1987 field 3 (processing code.)
                                                  Map to EMV tag ‘0x9c’
                                                  Ex.
                                                  “00” – purchase,
                                                  “01” – cash advance,
                                                   “09” – purchase with cashback,
                                                   “20” – refund.
                                                  The actual values to be used are defined by
                                                  the relevant payment system.
                   <SUB>            1             <1A>
                   [TranInfo]       2             Hexadecimal string to indicate the private
                                                  parameter “Transaction Info”. Map to private
                                                  tag ‘0x60000001’
                                                  Allowable values:
                                                   “80” - Cash (cash advance)
                                                   “40” - Goods
                                                   “20” - Services
                                                  “10” - Cashback
                   <SUB>            1             <1A>
                   [Account         2             Account Type, Map to EMV tag ‘0x5f57’
                   Type]                          Allowable values (ref. EMV v4.3 book3
                                                  Annex G)
                                                  “00” – Default
                                                  “10” – Savings
                                                  “20” – Cheque / Debit
                                                  “30” – Credit.
                   <SUB>            1             <1A>
                   [Force Online]   1             ‘1’: Force this transaction to be authorized
                                                  online.   Valid only if the terminal
                                                  configuration enables the capability to support
                                                  online authorization.
                                                  ‘0’: Run the default EMV authorization flow
                   <SUB>            1             <1A>
                   [Encrypted       16 or 32      Optional, TDES session keythat is used to
                   Session key]     or 48         encrypt the PIN entry when CVM asks for
                                                  online PIN verification.


===== PDF PAGE 203 =====

                                                       If the CVM asks for online PIN verification
                                                       but this session key is not entered, the A10-P
                                                       will ignore the PIN entry request and indicate
                                                       that no PIN is entered in the TVR register.
                      <SUB>              1             <1A>,Optional, only if [DO] exists
                      DO                 Var.          Data Object, format as below
                      <ETX>              1             <03>
                      [LRC]              1             Checksum


       Format Codes for Data Object Elements:
    Format                                                      Description
   1            a - Alphabetic data (a ~z, A~Z)
   2            b - unsigned binary numbers or bit combinations
   3            an - Alphanumeric data       (a ~z, A~Z, 0~9)
   4            ans - Alphanumeric Special data (Characters defined in ISO8859)
   5            cn - Compressed numeric data (0~9, left justify, pad hexadecimal ‘F’s. Ex. 12 34 56 12 3F FF)
   6            n - Numeric data (0~9, right justify, pad leading hexadecimal zeroes. Ex. 00 00 00 01 23 45)
   7            var - Variable data (Any bit combination)


       Notes on Data Objects field:
       If the data format is ‘2’ (binary), ‘5’ (compressed numeric), ‘6’ (numeric) or ‘7’ (variable data), the [value]
       corresponding to each format cannot be sent with the format directly in message T81. It shall be
       converted to a hexadecimal string. A pad character has to be added at the end if this tag# has odd
       characters. The pad char ‘F’ is used for format ‘cn’, and ‘0’ is used for formats ‘b’ or ‘n’.


       Example:
       Balance Read Before Gen AC:              000000 (Numeric)
       Balance Read After Gen AC:               000000 (Numeric)
       [DO]:
       DF8104<FS>6<FS>000000<SUB>DF8105<FS>6<FS>000000


===== PDF PAGE 204 =====

    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction               A10-P

                    Message T81                    →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                          Message T12/T16/T62
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message SIG                Signals to the Terminal
    Format:           <STX>SIG[Control][PktNo][SeqID][DataType][Timeout]
                      <SUB>[Data]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            Initiated by Device during EMV transaction for external user interface request or send
                      DET signal. The terminal should handle the request with Display and Keypad, or
                      receive and match DET to get DEK, then return the result to the Device within the
                      timeout.


    Message element:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field          Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>              1             <02>
                   SIG                3             Message ID
                   [Control]          1             ‘0’: (transmitter side) more packets follow.
                                                    ‘1’: (transmitter side) the last packet.
                                                    ‘2’: (receiver side) ready for the next packet.
                                                    ‘3’: (receiver side) acknowledge the last
                                                    packet.
                   [PktNo]            1             ‘0’ ~ ‘9’, The packet number (increment on
                                                    transmitter side).
                   [SeqID]            2             “00” ~ “FF”, the sequence number increments
                                                    on each SIG packet on both sides for
                                                    handshaking purpose.
                                                    Reset on power-on, rollover to “00” after
                                                    reaching “FF”.
                   [DataType]         2             “00”: Clear screen.
                                                    “03”: Display text and get function key.
                                                    “04”: Function key input (response of 03.)
                                                    “07”: Display and select Application ID.
                                                    “08”: Selected AID (response of 07.)
                                                    “09”: Display and select language.
                                                    “0A”: Selected language (response of 09.)
                                                    “0B”: Data Exchange Kernel (DEK Signal)
                                                    “0C”: Data Exchange Terminal (DET Signal,
                                                    response of 0B)
                                                    “FF”: Host error or times out.
                   [Timeout]          2             Hex string represents the waiting time for


===== PDF PAGE 206 =====

                                                      response, in seconds. Available value: “01” ~
                                                      “FE”.
                                                      “00”: No waiting time and no need response
                                                      SIG packet.
                                                      “FF”: Wait forever for responds.
                    <SUB>               1             <1A> as delimiter
                    [Data]              Var.          Data field, see description below.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Check value


    [Data] format for type “00” (clear screen) (reserved word):

                    Field           Length                          Value and description

           [Data]                   0          The “clear screen” have an empty data field.
                                               The terminal should clear its display.


    [Data] format for type “03” (display text and get function key) (reserved word):

                    Field           Length                          Value and description

           [Data]                   Var.       The message field includes ASCII text messages to be shown
                                               on external UI.
                                               The terminal should display the message and collect a single
                                               function key (ENTER / CANCEL,) or responds a “type FF”
                                               packet for error situation.


    [Data] format for type “04” (function key input) (reserved word):

                    Field           Length                          Value and description

           [Data]                   1          ‘0’: ENTER key pressed.
                                               ‘1’: CANCEL key pressed.


    [Data] format for type “07” (display and select Application ID) (reserved word):

                 Field          Length                              Value and description

        [Data]                  Var.        [AppName1]<FS>[AppName2]<FS>…
                                            Each AppName is a string that identifies application in the IC card.
                                            The terminal should parse the AID list and allow the user to select
                                            one AID for following EMV transaction, or responds a “type FF”
                                            packet for error situation.


===== PDF PAGE 207 =====

    [Data] format for type “08” (selected AID) (reserved word):

                    Field          Length                         Value and description

           [Data]                  Var       The exact AID string that user chose.


    [Data] format for type “09” (display and select language) (reserved word):

                    Field          Length                         Value and description

           [Data]                  Var.      [LangID1]<FS>[LangID2]<FS>…
                                             Each LangID is a two-character ISO639-1 language code, or
                                             “Default” that allow the terminal to determine its preferred
                                             language.
                                             The terminal should parse the LangID list and allows the
                                             user to select one supported language for following EMV
                                             transaction, or responds a “type FF” packet for error
                                             situation.


    [Data] format for type “0A” (selected language) (reserved word):

                    Field          Length                         Value and description

           [Data]                  Var       The exact language ID string that user chose.


    [Data] format for type “0B” (Data Exchange Kernel (DEK Signal)):

                    Field          Length                         Value and description

           [Data]                  Var       <SUB>[Tag#]<FS>[Length Code]<FS>[Value]...


    [Data] format for type “0C” (Data Exchange Terminal (DET Signal)):

                    Field          Length                         Value and description

           [Data]                  Var       <SUB>[Tag#]<FS>[Length Code]<FS>[Value]...


    [Data] format for type “FF” (host error or timed out) (reserved word):

                    Field          Length                         Value and description

           [Data]                  1         ‘0’: Timeout happened.
                                             ‘1’: User aborted.
                                             ‘2’: Other error on the host side.


===== PDF PAGE 208 =====

    Message Flow (signals that does not require response):
                            HOST               Direction                 A10-P

                   T61                             →
                                                          Message SIG
                   <ACK> if LRC ok.                →
                   <NAK> if LRC error.


    Message Flow (signals that require response):
                            HOST               Direction                 A10-P

                   T61                         →
                                                          Message SIG (request)
                   <ACK> if LRC ok.            →
                   <NAK> if LRC error.
                   Message SIG (response)      →
                                                          <ACK> / <NAK>


===== PDF PAGE 209 =====

    Example:
    This example is the captured transmission for AID / language selection by card holder. The terminal
    configuration needs to be modified to enable these features. <ACK> / <NAK> characters are omitted. For
    MCL data exchange:
                             HOST               Direction              A10-P

                   T61                             →
                                                           SIG10000BFF<1C>FF810400DF8

                   SIG10010C00<1C>DF8              →
                   1120182

                   1100100DF81100100DF
                   81100100DF81100100D
                   F81100100DF81100100
                   DF81100100DF8110010
                   0DF81100100DF811001
                   00DF81100101DF81120
                   55A5F209F12
                                                           SIG10000BFF<1C>FF81040D5A
                                                            0854133390000015139F1200
                   SIG10010C00<1C>                 →
                                                           T620A1


    Note: SIG occurs only between T61 and T62. The rest of the transaction process after this is according
    to other situations (e.g. T71, T63, T65).

Overall Contactless EMV Level 2 transaction flow reference

    A10-P provides one complete EMV Level 2 transaction with online or offline approval by various possible
    command flows. To force the A10-P to leave the command flow, you can either press the [CANCEL] key
    or send message T6C to the A10-P.


Ref. 1 Packet command flow for transaction with offline approval


                            HOST               Direction               A10-P

                    T61 (Start Transaction)        →
                                                           T62 (Start Transaction Response)
                                                   
                                                           [Result] = ‘Y1’ or ‘Z1’
                    T65 (Get online data
                                                   →
                    from A10-P)
                                                           T66 (Online authorization data
                                                   
                                                           response)
                    T63 (Get Transaction
                                                   →
                    Result’s Data)
                                                           T64 (Get Transaction Result Data
                                                   
                                                           Response)


===== PDF PAGE 211 =====

Ref. 2 Packet command flow for transaction with online approval


                            HOST               Direction               A10-P

                    T61 (Start Transaction)        →
                                                           T62 (Start Transaction Response)
                                                   
                                                           [Result] = ‘A1’
                    T65 (Get online data
                                                   →
                    from A10-P)
                                                           T66 (Online authorization data
                                                   
                                                           response)
                    T63 (Get Transaction
                                                   →
                    Result’s Data)
                                                           T64 (Get Transaction Result Data
                                                   
                                                           Response)
                    Go Online
                    T71     (Send    Online
                                                   →
                    Authorized Code)
                                                          T62 (Start Transaction Response)
                    T63 (Get Transaction
                                                   →
                    Result’s Data)
                                                           T64 (Get Transaction Result Data
                                                   
                                                           Response)


===== PDF PAGE 212 =====

Section 8           MIFARE Card Messages
    This chapter describes the commands of the MIFARE cards. The A10-P supports the MIFARE classic,
    Ultralight and DESFire cards. All MIFARE cards are compliant to the ISO/IEC 14443-1, 2 and 3
    standards. The MIFARE DESFire card supports the ISO/IEC 14443-4 protocol (also called “T=CL”
    protocol).


Message P01                Enable/Disable MIFARE
    Format:           <STX>P01[Flag]<EXT>[LRC] (request frame)
                      <STX>P01[Result][Reason]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Fixed 7 bytes for the request frame and variable for the response frame.
    Usage:            Enable or Disable the MIFARE.


    Message element:
                                           Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field           Length                   Value and description

                   <STX>               1             <02>
                   P01                 3             Message ID

                                                     ‘1’: Fail.

                                                     ‘1’: Format error.
                                                     ‘2’: Sequence error.
                   <ETX>               1             <03>
                   [LRC]               1             Checksum

Message P03                MIFARE Anti-collision
    Format:           <STX>P03<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)
                      <STX>P03[Result][UID][Reason]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes for the request frame
                      Variable for the response frame.
    Usage:            This command is used to get the card’s UID. When many cards are in the RFID field,
                      the A10-P will only return the UID from one card.

                         Field           Length                   Value and description

                   <ETX>               1             <03>

                   P03                 3             Message ID
                   [Result]            1             ‘0’: Success.
                                                     ‘1’: Fail.
                   [UID]               Var.          Optional: if [Result] = ‘0’
                                                     The UID length is 8, 14 or 20 bytes.
                                                     Format: hexadecimal string.
                   [Reason]            1             Optional: if [Result] = ‘1’
                                                     ‘1’: Format error.
                                                     ‘2’: Sequence error.
                                                     ‘3’: Anti collision fails.
                   <ETX>               1             <03>
                   [LRC]               1             Checksum

Message P07                MIFARE Classic Card Authentication
    Format:           <STX>P07[Sector number][Key type][key value]<ETX>[LRC]
                      (request frame 1)
                      <STX>P07[Sector number][Key number][key type]<ETX>[LRC]
                      (request frame 2)
                      <STX>P07[Result][Reason]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)
    Message length: variable.
    Usage:            Before any memory operation can be carried out, the card has to be selected and
                      authenticated.


    NOTE:             If the [Key type] and [Key value] is empty in the request frame 1, the A10-P will use key
                      value with “FFFFFFFFFFFF” and key type with ‘A’.


    Message element:
                                          Request1 frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field          Length                   Value and description

                   <STX>              1             <02>
                   P07                3             Message ID
                   [Sector            2             The sector number.
                   number]                          Decimal string: 00~39.
                   [Key type]         1             (optional)
                                                    Determine which card type to authenticate.
                                                    Format: ‘A’ or ‘B’.
                   [Key value]        12            (optional)
                                                    The card will be authenticated with this key
                                                    value.
                                                    Format: hexadecimal string.
                   <ETX>              1             <03>
                   [LRC]              1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 225 =====

                          Field           Length                   Value and description

                    <STX>               1             <02>
                    P07                 3             Message ID
                    [Sector             2             The sector number.
                    number]                           Decimal string: 00~39.
                    [Key number]        3             The key stored in the A10-P.
                                                      Decimal string: 000~255.
                    [Key type]          1             Determine which card type to authenticate.
                                                      Format: ‘A’ or ‘B’.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum


                                          Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field           Length                   Value and description

                    <STX>               1             <02>
                    P07                 3             Message ID
                    [Result]            1             ‘0’: Success.
                                                      ‘1’: Fail.
                    [Reason]            1             Optional: if [Result] = ‘1’
                                                      ‘1’: Format error.
                                                      ‘2’: Sequence error.
                                                      ‘3’: Key authentication fail.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum

Message P14                MIFARE Classic Card Value Operation
    Format:           <STX>P14[Block number][Op mode][Value][Transfer block]
                      <ETX>[LRC] (request frame)
                      <STX>P14[Result][Reason]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Fixed 13, 18 or 21 bytes for the request frame
                      Fixed 7 or 8 bytes for the response frame.
    Usage:            Value operation with block data.


    Message element:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field          Length                Value and description

                   <STX>              1             <02>
                   P14                3             Message ID
                   [Block             3             The block number.
                   number]                          Decimal string: 000~255.
                   [op mode]          1             Operation mode:
                                                    '0' = Create MIFARE Value block.
                                                    '1' = Decrement.
                                                    '2' = Increment.
                                                    '3' = Copy block.
                   [Value]            8             (Optional), if [op mode] != '3'
                                                    Value blocks allow performing electronic
                                                    purse functions.
                                                    Format: hexadecimal string ( signed integer ).
                                                    Ex : 1234567d = 00 12 D6 87
                   [Transfer          3             (Optional), if [op mode] != '0'
                   block]                           The block number.
                                                    Decimal string: 000~255.
                   <ETX>              1             <03>
                   [LRC]              1             Checksum

Message P19                I/O to MIFARE card with APDU format
    Format:            <STX>P19[C-APDU]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)
                       <STX>P19[Result][R-APDU][Reason]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: variable.
    Usage:             The command is used to send an APDU to the card where both data and an ISO status
                       are expected in the response.
    Note:              The APDU (Application Protocol Data Unit) format is defined in ISO/IEC 7816-4
                       chapter 5. Below briefly describes the command and response of the APDU.
                                                    C-APDU format
              CLA              INS             P1             P2             Lc      Data       Le
                                                    R-APDU format
                 Data (Optional)                                           SW1~SW2

                          Field           Length                   Value and description

                    [C-APDU]            Var.          Command APDU. The C-APDU format
                                                      please see Note.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum


                                          Response frame (A10-P to HOST)

                    [Result]            1             ‘0’: Success.
                                                      ‘1’: Fail.
                    [R-APDU]            Var.          Optional: if [Result] = ‘0’
                                                      The R-APDU format please see Note.
                    [Reason]            1             Optional: if [Result] = ‘1’
                                                      ‘1’: Format error.
                                                      ‘2’: Sequence error.
                                                      ‘3’: APDU fail.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>

Section 9           Online Transaction Messages with Master/Session

Keys (MK/SK)

Message 70            PIN entry request (MK/SK)
    Format:           <STX>70.[Account]<FS>[session key][Amount]<FS>[timeout]<ETX>[LRC]
                       (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             Display prompt to invite user to input PIN.
                       The PIN pad polls for keypad inputs until one of following criteria happens:
                       [ENTER] key pressed;             PIN pad validates input and sends message 71.
                       [CAN] key pressed;               PIN pad aborts PIN entry and sends <EOT>.
                       Message 72 received;             PIN pad aborts PIN entry and sends <EOT>.
                       Timed out (session or single key idle for 60 seconds); Acts as [CAN] key.


    Notes:             User Interactive Sequence: The prompts on the LCD of each phase are as following.


                       After message 70 received                        Total Amount
                       (The display language can be selected)           $xxx.xx
                                                                        Enter PIN
                                                                        Push “ENTER”
                       After user presses first digit                   PIN:
                       (Each * represents one digit)                    ****
                       If [ENTER] pressed                               PIN PAD
                                                                        PROCESSING
                                                                        (Until [CLR] pressed or new command
                                                                        received, can be turned off by message Z7)
                       If [CAN] pressed                                 Transaction
                                                                        Cancelled
                                                                        (Return to idle prompt after 3 seconds, can be
                                                                        turned off by message Z7)


===== PDF PAGE 258 =====

                       PIN length: According to the ANSI X9.8 standard, the length of the PIN should be
                       between 4 to 12 digits. If the user inputs less than 4 digits and then press ENTER,
                       A10-P will beep for error and continue to wait for the user’s input. After the user inputs
                       the 13th character, the A10-P will beep for error, conserves the PIN characters 1st to
                       12th, and wait for the ENTER.


                       This message has implemented the countermeasure to prevent an exhaustive PIN
                       determination attack. See Appendix A for details.


                       Master key must be selected before starting the transaction: A10-P will warn and
                       refuse message 70 if message 08 was not issued before.


                       The key and algorithm that used to encrypt the PIN block:


                                    Session Key            8 bytes (16 chars) or    24 bytes (48 chars)
                       Active Master Key                   16 bytes (32 chars)      Session key
                                                           Session key
                           TDES “K0” (24 bytes)            Use MK to decrypt SK     Use MK to decrypt SK
                                                           Use SK to encrypt PIN    Use SK to encrypt PIN
                           TDES “P0” (24 bytes)            SK must be “0”           SK must be “0”
                                                           Use MK to encrypt PIN    Use MK to encrypt PIN
                           TDES “K0” (16 bytes)            Use MK to decrypt SK     Error.
                                                           Use SK to encrypt PIN
                       TDES “P0” (8 or 16 bytes)           SK must be “0”           Error.
                                                           Use MK to encrypt PIN


===== PDF PAGE 259 =====

    Message element:
                                           Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field          Length                 Value and description

                    <STX>              1            <02>
                    70                 2            Message ID
                    .                  1            <2E>, delimiter
                    [Account]          8..19        Card account number
                    <FS>               1            <1C>, field separator
                    [session key]      32 or 48     Session key encrypted by the active master
                                                    key. The operating mode to decrypt the
                                                    session key will be ECB.
                                                    Format: hexadecimal string.
                                                    This field should be all zeros if the active
                                                    master key is usage “P0”
                    [Amount]           4 to 12      Amount of goods to be displayed on the
                                                    A10-P.
                                                    Accepts digit character (‘0’ ~ ‘9’), minus (‘-‘),
                                                    comma (‘,’) and decimal point (‘.’)
                    <FS>               1            (optional) <1C>, field separator
                    [timeout]          1            (optional) ASCII characters from ‘1’ to ‘9’
                                                    which is the timeout value in units of 30
                                                    seconds. Default = 9x30 = 270 seconds.
                    <ETX>              1            <03>
                    [LRC]              1            Checksum
    Message flow:
                                HOST              Direction                    A10-P

                    Message 70                       →
                                                               <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                               <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                     
                                                               <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                               Prompt user to enter PIN.
                                                               Message 71 or <EOT> when input
                                                     
                                                               times out or [CAN] is pressed
                    <ACK> (Good echo)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                  →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                               Display “PIN PAD
                                                               PROCESSING” until [CLEAR] is
                                                               pressed or another message is


===== PDF PAGE 260 =====

                                                                 received.

Message 71               Encrypted PIN Block Response (MK/SK)
    Format:              <STX>71.[fkey][PIN len][fmt][PIN blk][LRC] (response frame)
                         <STX>71[error code]<ETX>[LRC] (error frame)


    Message length: Fixed 27 bytes for TDES PIN block and 6 bytes for error code frame.
    Usage:               Send the entered PIN to the host in encrypted format.


    Message element:
                                        Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field         Length                Value and description

                    <STX>             1             <02>
                    71                2             Message ID
                    .                 1             <2E> delimiter
                    [fkey]            1             Always ‘0’ (This field is kept to retain the old
                                                    model compatibility.)
                    [PIN len]         2             Decimal string representing PIN length in the
                                                    PIN block.
                                                    Available value: “04” to “12”, or “00” if Z62
                                                    allowing null PIN option is enabled.
                    [fmt]             2             “01” = TDES PIN block.
                    [PIN blk]         16            Encrypted PIN block
                                                    Format: hexadecimal string.
                                                    16 bytes: TDES
                    <ETX>             1             <03>
                    [LRC]             1             Checksum


                                           Error frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field         Length                Value and description

                    <STX>             1             <02>
                    71                2             Message ID
                    [Error code]      1             Code to indicate error (see next page)
                    <ETX>             1             <03>
                    [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 261 =====

    Message flow:
                             HOST                Direction                      A10-P

                    Message 70/Z60/Z62               →
                                                               <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                              <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                               <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                               Message 71 or <EOT> when input
                                                     
                                                               times out or [CAN] is pressed
                    <ACK> (Good echo)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                  →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                               Display processing prompt


    Error codes:
                   Code                                     Meaning
                     '0'    Null Account input field.
                     '1'    Key value error.
                            (session key value conflicted with the usage of the active master key,
                            or session key length longer than the active master key)
                     '2'    Account number shorter than 8 digits.
                     '3'    Account number longer than 19 digits.
                     '4'    Account number has character(s) other than '0'-'9'.
                     '5'    Session key format error.
                     '6'    Timeout value error
                     '7'    No more DES operation within 60 minutes (refer to Appendix A)
                     '8'    Amount string format error originated from Message 70
                            PIN count, Accept Null PIN flag, and Prompt string format error
                            originated from Message Z62
                     '9'    Active master key not exists
                     'A'    Active master key usage incorrect (non K0 or P0 key)
                     'B'    Key storage access error
                     'C'    Memory buffer allocation error
                     'E'    Data length error in a field.
                     'G'    Specified file not found or authentication error.


===== PDF PAGE 262 =====

Message 72               PIN Entry Cancel
    Format:              <STX>72<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 5 bytes.
    Usage:               Cancel the current transaction that is waiting for user’s input and return the A10-P to
                         the IDLE state. The commands that can be canceled by message 72 include 70, Z60,
                         Z62 (for both MK/SK and DUKPT), 60, 62, Z42, Z50, Q1, Q8, Q9, QK.
                         If the A10-P receives message 72 while processing the user input such as swipe card,
                         enter a PIN or keying in data, It will respond with <EOT> to acknowledge that the
                         operation is canceled.


    Message element:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field         Length                Value and description

Message 7G               PIN Change Request (MK/SK)
    Format:              <STX>7G.[Account]<FS>[session key][Amount]<FS>[timeout]
                         <ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable 36 to 51 bytes (max. 67 bytes for TDES session key).
    Usage:               Display prompt and accept customer PIN input for PIN change process. The PIN Pad
                         will then wait till the PIN entered and [ENTER] key is pressed twice to make sure the
                         both entered PIN are matched. After ENTER key is pressed, the string “PIN PAD” and
                         “PROCESSING” will be displayed until the CLEAR key is pressed. During this period,
                         the PIN Pad will not process any message other than the CANCEL message
                         (message 72).


    NOTE:                The data format of Message 7G is exactly the same as Message 70. And the response
                         message is the same as Message 71 Encrypted PIN Block Response.

                    <STX>             1             <02>
                    7G                2             Message ID

                    [Account]         8..19         Card account number
                    <FS>              1             <1C>, field separator
                    [session key]     16 or 32      Working key encrypted using selected master
                                                    key. 32-characters session key produces
                                                    TDES encrypted PIN block with EDE order.
                                                    Format: hexadecimal string.
                    [Amount]          4..8          Amount of goods to be displayed on PIN Pad.
                                                    (Maximum value of integer amount is 99999)
                    <FS>              1             (optional) <1C>, field separator
                    [timeout]         1             (optional) ASCII character from ‘1’ to ‘9’
                                                    which is the timeout value in the unit of 30
                                                    seconds. Default = 9x30 = 270 seconds.
                    <ETX>             1             <03>
                    [LRC]             1             Checksum

Message Z64                Query Key Check Value (KCV)
    Format:            <STX>Z64[KeyID]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 7 bytes.
    Usage:             This message will export the KCV of the specified master key.
                       KCV is calculated as following:
                       1. Use [KeyID] specified key as encryption key.
                       2. Use "0000000000000000" (8 bytes zero) as data.
                       3. If the encrypt key is single length (8 bytes), use TDES algorithm to encrypt the data.
                       4. Take leftmost 3 bytes as KCV, output KCV as message Z65.
    Example:           TDES key "0123456789ABCDEF FEDCBA9876543210" will have KCV as "08D7B4".


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length                   Value and description

                    <STX>            1             <02>
                    Z64              3             Message ID
                    [KeyID]          1             '0' ~ '9', 'A' ~ 'G',
                                                   The ID of master keys.
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                              HOST              Direction                      A10-P

                    Message Z64                     →
                                                                 <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                                <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                 (<EOT> after 3 NAKs)
                                                                Message Z65
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 270 =====

Message Z65                Key Check Value Response
    Format:            <STX>Z65[KeyID][KCV]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Variable. 13 bytes for KCV, or 8 bytes for error code.
    Usage:             This message is the response of Z64.
                       If [KeyID] specified in Z64 is pointing to a valid master key, the KCV will be sent.
                       Otherwise a question mark '?' will be sent.


    Message element:
                                       Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field        Length                   Value and description

                    <STX>            1             <02>
                    Z64              3             Message ID
                    [KeyID]          1             '0' ~ '9', 'A' ~ 'G',
                                                   The ID of master keys.
                    [KCV]            6 or 1        Success: 6 characters of KCV.
                                                   Fail: '?'.
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Message flow:
        Refer to message Z64.


===== PDF PAGE 271 =====

Message Z66                Message Authentication Code (MAC) Request
    Format:            <STX>Z66[PktType][SeqNo][KeyId]<FS>[SessionKey]<FS>
                       [SecKeyId]<FS>[Message] <ETX> [LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable 14 to 270 bytes.
    Usage:             This message is used to generate the MAC codes according to the algorithm specified
                       in ANSI X9.19 (ISO 9797-1). User can send ASCII strings or hexadecimal strings to
                       the A10-P by message Z66 to generate its MAC. User can also breakdown a long
                       message into multiple Z66 messages with ascending sequence numbers to generate a
                       MAC.


    Notes:             Message Length: A onetime message that can be up to 224 characters (equal to 112
                       bytes when send as a hexadecimal string because 2 characters form 1 byte). Multiple
                       messages can have sequence numbers from 00 to 99. Thus the maximum capacity of
                       message Z66 is 22,400 characters (or 11,200 bytes in binary mode).
                       Multiple messages: When using multiple messages, [KeyId], [SessionKey] and
                       [SecKeyId] must be the same. [Message] must be a multiple of 8 characters (or 16
                       characters in binary mode), or else A10-P will generate a wrong MAC.
                       MAC algorithm: A10-P generates the TDES MAC according to ISO9797-1 algorithm 3.
                       (Padding with 0s. Initial vector = 0. Refer to Appendix A point 10 for the algorithm
                       details.)
                       Session Key: The value of the session key determines the usage of the specified
                       master keys.
                        Usage of 1st      Usage of 2nd     Value of session key   MAC Key
                        Key ID            Key ID
                        “K0”              N/A              Non-zero               Session key (decrypted by
                                                                                  master key specified by [KeyID]
                                                                                  using TDES ECB mode.)
                        “M3”              N/A              Zero                   Master key specified by
                        (mode G)                                                  [KeyId].
                                                                                  If the specified key is mode ‘V’,
                                                                                  this is for the MAC verification
                                                                                  and cannot be used to generate
                                                                                  the MAC for message Z66 .
                        “M1”              “M1”             Zero                   Master key specified by
                        (mode G)                                                  [KeyId] as left key, and
                                                                                  master key specified by
                                                                                  [SecKeyId] as right key.


===== PDF PAGE 272 =====

    Message element:
                                           Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field          Length                  Value and description

                    <STX>              1             <02>
                    Z66                3             Message ID
                    [PktType]          1             '4' = ASCII last or only packet.
                                                     '5' = ASCII first or middle of multiple packets.
                                                     '6' = Binary last or only packet.
                                                     '7' = Binary first or middle of multiple
                                                     packets.
                    [SeqNo]            2             '00' to '99'; for onetime only packet, set to 00.
                    [KeyId]            1             (Optional) Master key to use, range
                                                     = 'B' to 'E'. If this   field is blank, the MAC
                                                     master key will be the selected key 0 ~ 9.
                    <FS>               1             <1C>, field separator
                    [SessionKey]       32            Session key to be decrypted bythe Master key
                                                     pointed by [KeyId].
                                                     Format: hexadecimal string.
                                                     This field should be all zeros if the selected
                                                     key is usage “M3”
                    <FS>               1             <1C>, field separator
                    [SecKeyId]         1             (Optional) If first [KeyId] points to key with
                                                     “K0” or “M3” usage, this field should be
                                                     omitted.
                    <FS>               1             <1C>, field separator
                    [Message]          1-224         ASCII string or Hexadecimal string to be
                                                     MACed.
                    <ETX>              1             <03>
                    [LRC]              1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 273 =====

                    (Seq'00' and types 5,7)

                                                            Message Z67 (with MAC)
                    <ACK> / <NAK> /
                                                    →
                    <EOT>

Message Z67                Message Authentication Code (MAC) Response
    Format:            <STX>Z67[status][MAC]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Fixed 7 (status only) or 23 (with MAC) bytes.
    Usage:             The A10-P generated MAC              response. It contains the status codes or MAC.


    Message element:
                                        Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                      HOST              Direction                             A10-P

           <STX>                        1             <02>
           Z67                          3             Message ID
           [status]                     1             '0'=Success, MAC follows
                                                      '1'=Ready for the next Z66 packet, used in multiple
                                                      messages.
                                                      '2'=Sequence numbers out of order
                                                      '3'=Master key specified in [KeyId] not exists, or
                                                      range unacceptable (id 0 to A), or usage not “K0”,
                                                      “M3.
                                                      '4'=Master key specified in [SecKeyId] is
                                                      unreasonable.
                                                      '5'=[Message] length has error (too long, zero length,
                                                      or not an even number in binary mode)
                                                      '6'=[PkyType] flag has invalid value

                                                      '8'=[SessionKey] invaild
                                                      ‘A’=Session key is incompatible to the usage of the
                                                      specified master key.

                                                      zeros, if MK’s usage is “K0”, SK should not be

                                                      ‘B’ =Fatal error (malloc and DES error)
           [MAC]                        16            Calculated MAC, optional if status = 0.
           <ETX>                        1             <03>
           [LRC]                        1             Checksum


    Message flow:
        Refer to message Z66
