Section 10              Online Transaction Messages Secret (MK/SK)
Message 20              Load Secret Master Key
    Format:             <STX>20[key Opt][key value]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 22 bytes (DES key) or 38 bytes (TDES key).
    Usage:              This message loads secret master key (Secret MK) to the PIN pad. Secret MK
                        encrypts all other secret session keys (Secret SK); it cannot be used to encrypt PIN
                        block directly. If PIN pad already have Secret MK loaded and user want to update it,
                        new Secret MK can be sent to PIN pad by encrypted mode. When [key opt] set to 1,
                        PIN pad will decrypt the key value by previous Secret MK.


    NOTE 1:             When the [key opt] is set to 1, the decrypt algorithm depends on the key length of the
                        previous secret MK.
                        For example: If resident secret MK is double-length, the [key value] will be TDES
                        decrypted in ECB mode; otherwise [key value] will be DES decrypted.
    NOTE 3:             Loading a new secret MK will erase all resident secret SK.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   20                2             Message ID
                   [key Opt]         1             0 – cleartext key
                                                   1 – the key is encrypted by the previous key
                   [key value]       16 or 32      Hexadecimal string for key value
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 276 =====

    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction                    A10-P

                    Message 20                     →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                           <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                   
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs or key opt
                                                           error.)
                                                          Message 20 (echo frame)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs or         →
                    Good LRC but incorrect
                    key echoed)
                                                           (If PIN pad received <ACK>,
                                                          save the key.)
                                                           <EOT>

Message 21               Load Secret Session Key
    Format:              <STX>21[key ID][key value]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 22 bytes (DES key) or 38 bytes (TDES key).
    Usage:               This message loads secret session key (Secret SK) to the PIN pad. When loading the
                         secret SK, the secret MK must exists and the key id should be specified for key
                         storage. HOST should then send an <ACK> to confirm or <EOT> to cancel this key
                         loading process if the LRC is good but key value echoed is incorrect. Follow the
                         standard <NAK> process if an invalid LRC is detected.


    NOTE:                The secret SK decryption method depends on the length of the secret MK. If the secret
                         MK is a double length key, [key value] will be TDES decrypted in ECB mode.
                         Otherwise it will be decrypted by single DES.

                    [key value]       16 or 32      Hexadecimal string for encrypted secret SK.
                    <ETX>             1             <03>
                    [LRC]             1             Checksum

                    Good LRC but incorrect

Message 22              PIN entry request (Secret MK/SK)
    Format:             <STX>22[Account]<FS>[SSKid][Amount]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable 15 to 40 bytes
    Usage:              This message shows system standard PIN entry prompt, read user's PIN entry, then
                        create and encrypt PIN block via given account and secret SK. While waiting user's
                        input, PIN Pad will not process any message other than the CANCEL message
                        (message 72).
    NOTE:               Aborting transaction: Press [CLR] button to reset the PIN input and [CAN] button to abort
                        the transaction.
                        PIN length: According to ANSI X9.8 standard, the length of PIN should between 4 to 12
                        digits. If user inputs less than 4 digits and press ENTER, PIN pad will beep for error
                        and continue to wait for user’s input. When user inputs 13th character, PIN pad will
                        beep for error, conserves PIN character 1st to 12th, and wait for ENTER.
                        Triple DES capability: If specified secret SK is double length TDES key, the PIN block will
                        be TDES encrypted in EDE order.
                        Amount display: If the amount field is omitted. PIN Pad will not display amount prompt,
                        only basic PIN entry prompt will be displayed.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   22                2             Message ID
                   [Account]         8 to 19       Card account number
                   <FS>              1             <1C>, field separator
                   [SSKid]           1             0 – 9 Secret SK id.
                   [Amount]          4 to 14       [Optional] Amount of goods to be displayed
                                                   on PIN Pad.
                                                   (Maximum value of integer amount is
                                                   999.999.999.99). PIN Pad will not check the
                                                   content in this field.
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 279 =====

    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction                A10-P

                    Message 22                     →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                           <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                   
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           Prompt user to enter PIN.
                                                          Message 71
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           Display “PIN PAD
                                                           PROCESSING”until [CAN] key
                                                           is pressed or another message
                                                           received.

Message 24              PIN entry request with customized prompt (Secret MK/SK)
    Format:             <STX>24[Account]<FS>[SSKid][minPIN][maxPIN][null
                        flag][prompt1]<FS>[prompt2]<FS>[proc prompt]<ETX>[LRC]
                        (request frame)


    Message length: Variable 26 to 82 bytes.
    Usage:              This message shows PIN entry prompt specified by [prompt1] and [prompt2], read
                        user's PIN entry, then create and encrypt PIN block via given account and secret SK.
                        After encrypted PIN block sent to HOST, PIN pad will show [proc prompt] on screen
                        until [CAN] pressed or other message issued. While waiting user's input, PIN Pad will
                        not process any message other than the CANCEL message (message 72).


    NOTE:               Aborting transaction: Please refer to message 22.
                        PIN length: Message 24 can specify "minimum PIN" and "maximum PIN" counts, also
                        accepts null PIN (press [ENTER] directly without enter any digits). Refer to message
                        element for its range.
                        Triple DES capability: Please refer to message 22.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                 Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   24                2             Message ID
                   .                 1             <2E>, delimiter
                   [Account]         8 to 19       Card account number
                   <FS>              1             <1C>, field separator
                   [SSKid]           1             0 – 9 Id of secret session key.
                   [minPIN]          2             00, 04 .. 12 maximum PIN length.
                                                   ('00' only available when [null flag] set to 'Y').
                   [maxPIN]          2             00, 04 .. 12 maximum PIN length.
                                                   ('00' only available when [null flag] set to 'Y').
                   [null flag]       1             ‘Y’ Null PIN allowed
                                                   ‘N’ Null PIN not allowed
                   [prompt1]         1 to 16       Prompt displayed before any key is pressed,
                                                   alternate with prompt2
                   <FS>              1             <1C>, field separator
                   [prompt2]         1 to 16       Prompt displayed before any key is pressed,
                                                   alternate with prompt1
                   <FS>              1             <1C>, field separator


===== PDF PAGE 283 =====

                    [proc prompt]    1 to 16       Prompt displayed after PIN is entered
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                             HOST               Direction                  A10-P

                    Message 24                      →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                             Message 71
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              Display [proc prompt] until
                                                              CANCEL pressed or another
                                                              message received.


===== PDF PAGE 284 =====

Section 11             Prompt Control and Data Entry Messages
The display prompts of the A10-P are built-in and digitally signed with the A10-P application to ensure it’s
integrity and authenticity.
These commands have fixed display prompts:
⚫    Start IC card transaction (T11, T15, T33)
⚫    Start RFID transaction (T61, Q8)
⚫    Swipe Magnetic Stripe Card (Q1)
⚫    Combined card transaction (Q9, QK)
⚫    Standard PIN entry (70)
These commands have selectable display prompts:
⚫    Non-PIN data entry (Z42, Z50): The prompt can be selected from Appendix G (Z2/Z3 authenticated
     mode)
⚫    PIN entry with external prompts (Z60)
⚫    PIN entry with customized prompts (Z62)
The host application can also command A10-P to display any string on the LCD, but if the prompt string is not
MAC authenticated, the PIN entry or data entry function will be disabled.


Z2 / Z3 Authenticated mode with fixed prompt
    To enable messages Z42 and Z50, the user has to issue Z2 / Z3 messages with a prompt ID supported
    by the A10-P (See Appendix G). These prompts are verified to make sure users will not expose sensitive
    information (such as PIN) accidentally.


    For security reason, to issue the authenticated frame of Z2 / Z3 at the first time, the <SUB> flag is
    mandatory. After the Z2 authenticated mode is entered, the A10-P will accept two kinds of Z2 packet:
    1. Z2 packet in normal mode, without the <SUB> flagand contains only digits (0~9)
    2. Z2 packet in authenticated mode, without the <SUB> flag.
    For example, issuing Z2<GS>005<SUB> and Z2<GS>016 will show “PLEASE ENTER DRIVER
    LICENSE” on the screen.


Z2 / Z3 PIN entry mode with fixed prompt
    To enable message Z60, the user has to issue Z2 / Z3 messages with a prompt ID supported by the
    A10-P dedicated for PIN entry (See Appendix H). These prompts are verified to make sure users will not
    misunderstand the PIN entry request message as other non-sensitive data. Also prompt1 and prompt2 of
    message Z62 will be checked to see if they are listed in this prompt table. If not, the A10-P will reject
    message Z62.


    Messages other than Z2, Z3, Z42, Z50, and Z60, or any unsuccessful Z2 / Z3 messages (wrong prompt
    ID, format error, Z2 message including non decimal characters), will make the A10-P leaving the Z2 / Z3
    authenticated mode to avoid attack.


===== PDF PAGE 285 =====

Message Z0               Move Display Cursor
    Format:              <STX>Z0[YY]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 9 bytes.
    Usage:               Move the display cursor to the specified location. Z0 message is sent after the A10-P
                         receives the first Z2 message. Under the Z2-authenticated mode, the Z0 message is
                         also disabled.


    Message element:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field            Length               Value and description

                    <STX>             1              <02>
                    Z0                2              Message ID
                    [YY]              2              Y-coordinate, 01 ~ Max. line
                    <ETX>             1              <03>
                    [LRC]             1              Checksum


    Message flow:
                              HOST                 Direction                  A10-P

                    Message Z2                        →
                                                                <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                               <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                Display string.
                    Message Z0                        →
                                                               <ACK> / <NAK> / <EOT>
                                                                A10-P moves the cursor
                    Message Z2 (without
                                                      →
                    clear screen)
                                                               <ACK> / <NAK> / <EOT>
                                                                Display the 2nd string from the
                                                                coordinate specified by Z0.


===== PDF PAGE 286 =====

    Example
                            Command                                            Action
            <STX>Z2<SUB>PLEASE WAIT<ETX>                  Row 1: “PLEASE WAIT”
                                                          Row 2: (empty)
                                                          The cursor position is at 12th character of the
                                                          row 1 (right after ‘T’).
            <STX>Z002<ETX>                                The cursor position moved to the 1st
                                                          character of the row 2.
            <STX>Z2THANK YOU<ETX>                         Row 1: “PLEASE WAIT”
                                                          Row 2: “THANK YOU”
                                                          The cursor position is at 10th character of the
                                                          row 2 (after ‘U’).

Message Z1               Return to Idle State
    Format:              <STX>Z1<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 5 bytes.
    Usage:               When A10-P is displaying prompt messages (set by Z2 and Z3), this message resets
                         the display (display idle prompt) and logical state back to idle.
                         This message cannot be used to abort PIN entry or data entry session, use message
                         72 in such situation..
                         This command also reset the magnetic card swiping commands Q1, Q8, Q9.


    Message element:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field         Length                Value and description

                    <STX>             1             <02>
                    Z1                2             Message ID
                    <ETX>             1             <03>
                    [LRC]             1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                              HOST                Direction                 A10-P

                    Message Z1                       →
                                                               <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                              <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                               <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message Z2              Display String
    Format:             <STX>Z2<SUB>[string]<ETX>[LRC] (Request frame, normal)


    Message length: Variable, 6 to 70 bytes.
    Usage:              This message directs PIN pad to display specified string until following the situations:
                        ▪ User presses [CAN] key on the keypad.
                        ▪ PIN pad receives another message that changes the display contents (such as
                        message 70), or returns to the idle prompt (message Z1).
    Notes:              This message can be used to display authenticated prompts for data entry if following
                        criteria are satisfied:
                        ▪ If the specified string, after stripping non-alphabet characters (00h~40h, 5Bh~60h,
                        7Bh to 7Fh), is matching any one of the fixed prompts on the “Fixed prompts for Z2 /
                        Z3 authenticated mode”, the string will be treated as “authenticated”, and then
                        message Z42 and Z50 will be enabled.
                        ▪ If PIN pad is already displaying unauthenticated message, <SUB> will be needed in
                        order to accept authenticated message.
                        ▪ If <SUB> does not exist, and [string] contains digits (‘0’ to ‘9’) only, the authenticated
                        state of previous Z2 will be preserved.


    Message element:
                               Z2 request frame (normal mode) (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field           Length                  Value and description

                   <STX>               1             <02>
                   Z2                  2             Message ID
                   <SUB>               1             <1A> (optional)
                                                     If <SUB> exists, A10-P will clear the screen
                                                     contents.
                   [string]            0 ... 64      String to be displayed. The encoding can be
                                                     ASCII or UTF-8. The maximum length is 64
                                                     characters.
                   <ETX>               1             <03>
                   [LRC]               1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 289 =====

    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction                 A10-P

                    Message Z2                     →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           If format error, return <EOT>.
                                                   
                                                           Otherwise display the given string


    Examples:
    1. <STX>Z2<SUB>PLEASE ENTER PIN<ETX>
    2. PIN Pad will display “PLEASE ENTER PIN” on the screen, but since this message is not listed in the
          prompt table, Z42 will be restricted to function key, and Z50 will be rejected, and return to idle
          prompt.
    3. <STX>Z2<SUB>ENTER<ETX> → <STX>Z002<ETX> → <STX>Z2ACCOUNT NUMBER<ETX>
    4. PIN Pad will display “ENTER” at the first line, then display “ACCOUNT NUMBER” on the second line
          of screen. Message Z42 and Z50 will be allowed because both strings are listed on the prompt
          table (ID 024 and 001).
    5. <STX>Z2<SUB>ENTER <ETX> → <STX>Z002<ETX> → <STX>Z2YOUR PIN<ETX>
    6. The first Z2 is matching prompt ID 024 but the second does not match anything.
    7. In this case, PIN Pad will revoke the authentication previously enabled by the first Z2, thus Z42 will
          be restricted to function key, and Z50 will be rejected and back to the idle prompt.
    8. <STX>Z2<SUB>YOUR PIN <ETX>               → <STX>Z002<ETX> → <STX>Z2PLEASE<ETX>
    9. The first Z2 does not match any prompt ID, but the second Z2 matches ID 085.
    10. In this case, PIN Pad will refuse the second Z2 (response <EOT>) because it violates usage rule.
    11. PIN Pad will return to idle prompt, and Z42 / Z50 will be rejected because no valid prompt is
          displayed.


===== PDF PAGE 290 =====

Message Z2              Display String (Fixed Prompt ID)
    Format:             <STX>Z2[op][prompt_id]<SUB><ETX>[LRC] (request frame)
                        <STX>Z2[status]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Variable, 6 to 10 bytes.
    Usage:              This message directs PIN pad to display authenticated prompt string specified by ID
                        number, for data entry command (Z42, Z50), or PIN entry (Z60, 60) until the following
                        situations:
                        ▪ User presses [CAN] key on the keypad.
                        ▪ PIN pad receives another message that changes the display contents (such as
                        message 70), or returns to the idle prompt (message Z1).
    Note:
                        ▪ Z2 (prompt ID) can be followed by Z2 (string) which contains digits only.
                        ▪ The prompts with different usage should not be mixed. PIN pad will revoke all
                        authentications if user tries to change input mode without <SUB>.


    Message element:
                             Z2 request frame (fixed prompt ID) (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length                Value and description

                   <STX>              1            <02>
                   Z2                 2            Message ID
                   [op]               1            Defines which prompt table to be applied for
                                                   the search:
                                                   <1D> (GS): data entry (non-PIN) prompt.
                                                   <1E> (RS): PIN entry prompt.
                   Prompt ID          3            Prompt ID number that corresponds to the
                                                   authenticated prompt table.
                                                   Decimal string: 001 ~ 999.
                   <SUB>              1            <1A>
                                                    If <SUB> exists, the A10-P will clear the
                                                   screen contents.
                   <ETX>              1            <03>
                   [LRC]              1            Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 291 =====

                         Z2 response frame (authenticated mode) (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field            Length                Value and description

                    <STX>               1             <02>
                    Z2                  2             Message ID
                    [status]            1             ‘0’: OK
                                                      ‘1’: Prompt ID not supported.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                                HOST               Direction                      A10-P

                    Message Z2                         →
                                                                 <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                                <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                 <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                 Message Z2
                                                       
                                                                   (response frame)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                    →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                 Display string
                                                       
                                                                 <EOT> (if received <ACK>)

Message Z2              Display String with Authentication Code
    Format:             <STX>Z2<FS>[KeyId][MAC][op][string]<SUB><ETX>[LRC] (Request)
                        <STX>Z2[status]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Variable, 17 to 79 bytes.
    Usage:              This message directs PIN pad to display MAC authenticated strings, for data entry
                        commands (Z42, Z50), or PIN entry (Z60, 60) until the following situations:
                        ▪ User presses [CAN] key on the keypad.
                        ▪ PIN pad receives another message that changes the display contents (such as
                        message 70), or returns to the idle prompt (message Z1).
                        The MAC value is calculated from the following data concatenated as byte array:
                        ▪ [op] field.
                        ▪ All alphabets (‘A’ to ‘Z’, ‘a’ to ‘z’) in the [string] field. White spaces, punctuation marks
                        and digits are excluded.
                        ▪ <SUB> character if exists.
                        ▪ Padding ‘0’ (0x30) to the multiple of 8 bytes.
                        The algorithm is ISO 9797-1 algorithm 3.


    Message element :
                                  Z2 with MAC, request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field          Length                Value and description

                   <STX>                1            <02>
                   Z2                   2            Message ID
                   <FS>                 1            <1C>, field separator.
                   [KeyID]              1            ‘B’ ~ ‘E’, key to verify the MAC value. The
                                                     specified key must have usage ‘M3’ and mode
                                                     ‘V’.
                   [MAC]                8            Message authentication code of the following
                                                     message (including <SUB> if exists).
                   [op]                 1            <GS> (0x1D) for Non-PIN entry.
                                                     <RS> (0x1E) for PIN entry.
                   [string]             1 .. 62      ASCII string to be displayed
                   <SUB>                1            <1A> (optional)
                                                      If <SUB> exists, A10-P will clear the screen
                                                     contents and also reset the entry mode.
                   <ETX>                1            <03>
                   [LRC]                1            Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 293 =====

                                  Z2 with MAC, response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                         Field            Length                Value and description

                    <STX>               1             <02>
                    Z2                  2             Message ID
                    [status]            1             ‘0’: OK
                                                      ‘1’: MAC key ID error (out of ‘B’~’E’).
                                                      ‘2’: MAC key attribute error.
                                                      ‘3’: MAC value error.
                                                      ‘4’: Packet format error.
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                                HOST               Direction                      A10-P

                    Message Z2                         →
                                                                 <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                                <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                 <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                 Message Z2
                                                       
                                                                   (response frame)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                    →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                 Display string
                                                       
                                                                 <EOT> (if received <ACK>)


===== PDF PAGE 294 =====

Message Z3              Display Line Prompts
    Format:             <STX>Z3[count]<SUB>[prompt1]<FS>[prompt2..7]<ETX>[LRC]
                        (request frame)


    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:              This message directs PIN pad to display up to seven messages or prompts in rotation.
                        The display changes prompts in 3-second intervals until the following situations:
                        ▪ User presses [CAN] key on the keypad.
                        ▪ PIN pad receives another message that changes the display contents (such as
                        message 70), or returns to the idle prompt (message Z1).


                        This message can be used to display authenticated prompts for data entry if the
                        following criteria are satisfied:
                        If all prompts, after stripping non-alphabet characters (00h~40h, 5Bh~60h, 7Bh to 7Fh),
                        are matching the fixed prompts on the “Fixed prompts for Z2 / Z3 authenticated mode”,
                        or contains only non-alphabet characters, the string will be treated as “authenticated”,
                        and then message Z42 and Z50 will be enabled.
                        If PIN pad is already displaying unauthenticated message, <SUB> will be required in
                        order to accept authenticated message.


    Message element:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field          Length                  Value and description

                   <STX>              1             <02>
                   Z3                 2             Message ID
                   [Count]            1             Number of prompts to be displayed         (‘1’ to
                                                    ‘7’.)
                   <SUB>              1             <1A> (optional)
                                                     If <SUB> exists, A10-P will clear the screen
                                                    contents.
                   [Prompt1]          var           First string to be displayed. (ASCII or
                                                    UTF-8), maximum length is 16 characters.
                   <FS>               1             <1C>, field separator
                   [Prompt2..7]       var           Remaining strings to be displayed.
                                                    Note. <FS> is required between
                                                    messages
                   <ETX>              1             <03>
                   [LRC]              1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 295 =====

    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction                 A10-P

                    Message Z3                     →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           Display the specified prompts


    Examples:
    1.                 <STX>Z32<SUB>AMOUNT 1.99<FS>ENTER YOUR PIN<ETX>
                       PIN Pad will clear the screen, then display “AMOUNT 1.99” “ENTER YOUR PIN” on
                       the screen. But this message is not authenticated because it does not match any fixed
                       prompts for non-PIN entry. So message Z42 will be restricted to function key, and Z50
                       will be rejected. PIN Pad will return to idle prompt after Z42/Z50.


    2.                 <STX>Z33<SUB>CUSTOMER REF NO<FS>8287<FS>ENTER CASH BACK<ETX>
                       PIN Pad will clear screen then display “CUSTOMER REF NO”, “8287”, “ENTER CASH
                       BACK” in three lines. Since line 1 matches ID 016, line 2 contains all digits, and line 3
                       matches ID 030, this message will be evaluated as authenticated for non-PIN entry.
                       Message Z42 and Z50 will be allowed.


    3.                 <STX>Z32<SUB>ENTER<FS>YOUR PIN<ETX>
                       PIN Pad will clear screen and display “ENTER”, “YOUR PIN” in two lines. Line 1
                       matches ID 024 but line 2 does not match any fixed prompt, so this message will not
                       be evaluated as authenticated, Z42 will be restricted to function key, and Z50 will be
                       rejected.


    4.                 <STX>Z33<SUB>1. ENTER CARD #<FS>2. ENTER CUST #<FS>3. ENTER ID
                       #<ETX>
                       PIN Pad will clear screen and display “1. ENTER CARD #”, “2. ENTER CUST #”, “3.
                       ENTER ID #” in three lines. Line 1 matchs ID 031 after stripping non-alphabet
                       characters, line 2 and line 3 also match other IDs. So this message will be evaluated
                       as authenticated, Z42 / Z50 will be allowed.


    5.                 <STX>Z32ENTER SSN<FS>OR PHONE #<ETX>
                       PIN Pad will reject this message (response <EOT>) because it tries to enter the
                       authenticated display mode without clearing screen.


===== PDF PAGE 296 =====

Message Z3            Display Line Prompts with Authentication Code
    Format:            <STX>Z3<FS>[KeyID][MAC][count][op][prompt1]<FS>[prompt2..7]
                       <SUB><ETX>[LRC] (request frame)
                       <STX>Z3[status]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)
    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:             This message directs PIN pad to display up to seven MAC authenticated prompts in
                       rotation. The display changes prompts in 3-second intervals until the following
                       situations:
                       ▪ User presses [CAN] key on the keypad.
                       ▪ PIN pad receives another message that changes the display contents (such as
                       message 70), or returns to the idle prompt (message Z1).
                       The MAC value is calculated from the following data concatenated as byte array:
                       ▪ [op] field.
                       ▪ All alphabets (‘A’ to ‘Z’, ‘a’ to ‘z’) in the [string] field. White spaces, punctuation marks
                       and digits are excluded.
                       ▪ <FS> character if exists.
                       ▪ <SUB> character if exists.

                       2. A10-P will temporarily turn off the date/timescreen for the first Z2 message it
                       received. The A10-P will return to the idle mode and show the date/time screen after
                       the following possible scenarios: The key entry message command is finished (Z42,
                       Z50 or Z60), the [CAN] is pressed, or the message 72 is received.

===== PDF PAGE 301 =====

Example to use Z2 / Z3 with Authencation Code.
    [Example 1]
            a. Use message 02 (ANSI TR31 frame) to load the following key to position ‘B’:
                  BCDE90123456789ABCDE90123456789A, Usage = M3, Mode = V.
            b. Assume we want to clear the screen and display the following string for PIN entry: “AMOUNT
                  123456.78 ENTER YOUR PIN”.
            c.    The data for MAC generation (‘A’ to ‘Z’, ‘a’ to ‘z’ and ISO8859-15 high page characters from
                  0xBC to 0xFF, padded with ASCII 0):
                  <RS>AMOUNTENTERYOURPIN<SUB>0000
                    1E414D4F554E54454E544552594F555250494E1A30303030
                  The white spaces and digits are not counted into the MAC. This feature enables the acquirer
                  to issue PIN entry prompts with different amounts, but keeps the same MAC value.
            d. Use the key specified in step a to calculate ISO9797-1 algorithm 3 MAC.
                  The result is: C51401D727D761E2.
                  Take leftmost 4 bytes as MAC value: C51401D7.
            e. Send <02>Z2<1C>BC51401D7<1E>AMOUNT 123456.78 ENTER YOUR PIN<1A><03> to
                  A10-P, Then message Z60 can be issued to request PIN entry.
            f.    Send <02>Z2<1C>BC51401D7 <1A>AMOUNT 123.45 ENTER YOUR PIN<03> to A10-P to
                  see the same MAC applies to different amounts.


    [Example 2]
     1.    Use message 02 (ANSI TR31 frame) to load the following key to position ‘B’:
           6AC292FAA1315B4D8234B3A3D7D5933A, Usage = M3, Mode = V.
     2.    Assume we want to clear the screen and display for the non-PIN entry: “MESSAGE ONE 1.0” and
           “MESSAGE TWO 2.0”.
     3.    The data for the MAC generation (padded with ASCII 0):
           <GS>MESSAGEONE<FS>MESSAGETWO<SUB>0
             1D4D4553534147454F4E451C4D45535341474554574F1A30
     4.    Use the key specified in    step 1 to calculate the ISO9797-1 algorithm 3 MAC.
           Take the leftmost 4 bytes as the MAC value: 22C0BAD9.
     5.    Send <02>Z3<1C>B22C0BAD92<1D>MESSAGE ONE 1.0<1C>MESSAGE TWO 2.0<1A><03> to
           the A10-P.


===== PDF PAGE 302 =====

Message Z42                Read Key Code
    Format:            <STX>Z42[timeout]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable 7 to 9 bytes.
    Usage:             Once A10-P receives this command, it begins polling the functional key array until time
                       out happens as specified in [timeout].
                       If A10-P receives the Z2 / Z3 authenticated frame before message Z42, it will return
                       any key pressed by the user as ASCII key codes via message Z43. Else it will return
                       only the function key codes (F1, F2, F3, CAN, CLR, ENTER), and reject the numerical
                       keys (0 to 9). Multiple key presses or combined key presses will be discarded.
                       Abort input: Issue message 72 to abort the operation.
    Note:             Z2/Z3 required: Because Z42 will not show any message to prompt the user operation,
                       Z2 or Z3 should be issued before this command, or A10-P will send out <EOT> and
                       stops.


    Message element:
                                           Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field         Length               Value and description

                    <STX>              1           <02>
                    Z42                3           Message ID
                    [timeout]          1 to 3      ASCII character from 1 to 255, for example
                                                   “10” means 10 seconds of timeout.
                    <ETX>              1           <03>
                    [LRC]              1           Checksum


    Message flow:
                                HOST             Direction                  A10-P

                    Message Z2 or Z3                →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              Show prompt message
                    Message Z42                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                             Message Z43
                    <ACK>/<NAK>/<EOT>               →


===== PDF PAGE 303 =====

Message Z43                Read Key Code Response
    Format:            <STX>Z43[Keycode]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Fixed 7 bytes.
    Usage:             This is the response frame of message Z42.


    Message element:
                                       Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field        Length                   Value and description

                    <STX>            1             <02>
                    Z43              3             Message ID
                    [keycode]        1             ‘0’ to ‘9’
                                                   ASCII ‘A’ to ‘C’ denote 3 function keys.
                                                   ‘A’ = [F1]
                                                   ‘B’ = [F2]
                                                   ‘C’ = [F3]
                                                   ‘*’ = [CAN]
                                                   ‘#’ = [ENTER]
                                                   ‘/’ = [CLR]
                                                   ‘?’ means time out.
                    <ETX>            1             <03>
                    [LRC]            1             Checksum


    Message flow:
        Refer to message Z42


===== PDF PAGE 304 =====

Message Z50                String Entry Request
    Format:            <STX>Z50[echo flag][timeout][max entry]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable 10 to 12 bytes.
    Usage:             This message requests a string of key inputs to be entered from the PIN pad.
                       The PIN pad polls for keypad inputs until one of the following criteria happens:
                       ▪ [ENTER] key pressed; PIN pad validates input and sends message 71.
                       ▪ [CAN] key pressed;         PIN pad aborts PIN entry and sends <EOT>.
                       ▪ Message 72 received;       PIN pad aborts PIN entry and sends <EOT>
    ▪                  Times out (session or single key idle for 60 seconds).
    Note:-             To enable Z50 input session, send message Z2 or Z3 authenticated for data entry, (Z2
                       / Z3 with <GS> operand). Otherwise PIN pad will send <EOT> and return to idle state.
    -                  If input character count reaches [max entry], the PIN pad will stop collecting key input
                       and make error beeps. User can press [ENTER], [CLR], or [CAN].Request the user to
                       input a string from the keypad.
                       A10-P will wait for the keypad input and store the alphabet data string into the internal
                       buffer. The maximum length of the internal buffer is up to 49 characters.
                       User can    press [CLR] to clear the input buffer and start over the input , or press [CAN]
                       to cancel the input.
                       Using [0] and [MENU] for the period symbol and white space entries.
                       .Abort input: Issue message 72 to abort the operation.
    Note:              A message Z2 or Z3 with authenticated frame must be issued before sending message
                       Z50. Otherwise the A10-P will refuse to execute.
    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field         Length                  Value and description

                   <STX>             1             <02>
                   Z50               3             Message ID
                   [echo flag]       1             ‘0’: echo input as ‘*’
                                                   ‘1’: echo input as is
                                                   ‘2’: do not echo
                   [timeout]         3             ASCII characters from 1 to 255 to set the
                                                   timeout for each keypress. For example “010”
                                                   means 10 seconds of timeout after the last
                                                   keypress.
                   [max entry]       1 or 2        (optional) Maximum entry counts.
                                                   Range from 00 to 49 (or 0 to 49)
                   <ETX>             1             <03>
                   [LRC]             1             Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 305 =====

    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction                 A10-P

                    Message Z2 or Z3               →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           Show the prompt message
                    Message Z50                    →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           Message Z51
                                                          (or <EOT> when input is
                                                           cancelled)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message Z8               Set Idle Prompt
    Format:              <STX>Z8[prompt]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable: 5 to 21 bytes.
    Usage:               The A10-P will display an idle prompt when it is in IDLE state. The host can change
                         this idle prompt via message Z8. If the prompt field is filled with a null string, then the
                         A10-P will use the default prompt after receiving this null string command.

                    Message Z8                       →
                                                               <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                              <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                               <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                               Displays the idle prompt


===== PDF PAGE 309 =====

Section 12            ICC, SAM, MSR, and PCD Track Reading

Messages
    Messages listed in this section are used to:
    ⚫ Access IC card, SAM card directly (EMV power-up, power-down, APDU exchange.)
    ⚫ Read magnetic stripe card.
    ⚫ Read contactless smart card and output MSR track equivalent data.
    NOTE: By default, the MSR function is turned off until the user issues the Q4 command to enable it.


    I00 ~ I07: IC card manipulation.
    I11 ~ I17: SAM card manipulation.
    Q1 ~ Q7: Read magnetic stripe card and output control.
    Q8 ~ QJ: Read contactless smard card (MSR emulation) and output control.
    QK: Multiple interface (MSR, ICC, Contactless) card detection and reading.
    81: MSR data output
    83: Contactless MSR emulation data output.


    Depend on the firmware configuration (PTS only or PTS + SRED), the clear text data output is not
    available.


    PTS Only Configuration
    ⚫ I01 ~ I06 (ICC manipulation) is available.
    ⚫ Q6 (MSR operation control) does NOT support options for data encryption.
    ⚫ 81 (MSR card data) outputs clear text track data.
    ⚫ 83 (Contactless card data) outputs clear text track equivalent data.


    PTS + SRED Configuration
    ⚫ I01 ~ I06 (ICC manipulation) is NOT available.
    ⚫ Q6 (MSR operation control) supports data encryption (MK/SK and DUKPT) option.
    ⚫ QD (Contactless)
    ⚫ 81 (MSR card data) outputs encrypted (and optionally obfuscated) track data.
    ⚫ 83 (Contactless card data) outputs encrypted (and optionally obfuscated) track equivalent data.

Message I00               Query Primary Smart Card Presence
    Format:               <STX>I00<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)
                          <STX>I00[response]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes (request frame) / 7 bytes (response frame).
    Usage:                Upon receiving a query card packet, A10-P will check whether the card is present in
                          the primary card reader or not. If a card exists in the reader, A10-P will reply with the
                          code ‘F’. Otherwise, the code ‘0’ is used instead.


    Message element:
                                           Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                           Field         Length                Value and description

                    <STX>              1             <02>
                    I00                3             Message ID
                    <ETX>              1             <03>
                    [LRC]              1             Checksum


                                         Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                           Field         Length                Value and description

                    <STX>              1             <02>
                    I00                3             Message ID
                    [Response]         1             0: Smart card not exists.
                                                     F: Smart card exists
                    <ETX>              1             <03>
                    [LRC]              1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                               HOST               Direction                      A10-P

                    Message I00 (Request)             →
                                                                <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                               <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                Check card existence
                                                                Message I00 (response)
                    <ACK> (Good echo)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                   →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message I01               Primary Smart Card Cold Reset
    Format:               <STX>I01<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:                A10-P performs a cold reset on the active smart card. If the operation is successful,
                          A10-P will send out message I02 with the “Answer to Reset” (ATR) of the smart card to
                          the host. Otherwise, message I0F bearing the error code is sent back.
    NOTE: This message is not available in the PTS + SRED configuration.


    Message element:
                                           Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field          Length               Value and description

                    <STX>              1             <02>
                    I01                3             Message ID
                    <ETX>              1             <03>
                    [LRC]              1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                               HOST               Direction                   A10-P

                    Message I01                       →
                                                                <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                               <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                Smart card cold reset.
                                                      
                                                                Message I02 (ATR)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                   →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message I02               Primary Smart Card ATR Response
    Format:               <STX>I02[ATR]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Variable, depending on the specification of the smart card..
    Usage:                The message contains the “Answer to Reset” (ATR) of the smart card to be sent to the
                          host. In general, the ATR conforms to the ISO 7816-3 or EMV 4.3 Book 1 standard.
                          See these standards for more detailed information.
    NOTE: This message is not available in the PTS + SRED configuration.


    Message element:
                                         Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field          Length                Value and description

                    <STX>              1             <02>
                    I02                3             Message ID
                    [ATR]              var.          Answer to reset data.
                                                     Format: Hexadecimal string.
                    <ETX>              1             <03>
                    [LRC]              1             Checksum


    Message flow:
        Refer to message I01.

Message I06               Primary Smart Card C-APDU
    Format:               <STX>I06[C-APDU]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable 14 to 530 bytes.
    Usage:                The     C-APDU (application protocol data unit) is the data to be sent to the active smart
                          card. A10-P 's smart card reader will handle with base protocols such as T=0 and T=1,
                          and the programmer needs onlysending out the APDU. In general, A10-P will reply
                          message I07 (response APDU from card) to the host in 1 second. For any error, A10-P
                          will send back message I0F with an error code .
    NOTE: This message is not available in the PTS + SRED configuration.


    Message element:
                                             Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field            Length                Value and description

                    <STX>                1             <02>
                    I06                  3             Message ID
                    [C-APDU]             8..524        Smart card command APDU.
                                                       Format: hexadecimal string.
                    <ETX>                1             <03>
                    [LRC]                1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                                 HOST               Direction                  A10-P

                    Message I01                                   (Smart card power on and ATR
                                                       →
                                                                  response)
                    Message I06                         →
                                                                  <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                                 <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                  <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                  Communication with card.
                                                        
                                                                  Message I07 or I0F
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message I08            Smart Card Offline PIN Verification (EMV)
    Format:           <STX>I08[Mode]<FS>[Prompt & Amount]<ETX>[LRC] (Clear-text mode)
                      <STX>I08[Mode]<FS>[Prompt & Amount]<FS>RSA public Exp]<FS>[RSA
                      public Modulus]<ETX>[LRC] (Cipher-text mode)
    Message length: Variable.
    Usage:            User could apply message I01 ~ I06 to implement his EMV transaction flow. In EMV
                      transaction flow, user could apply this message to implement the offline PIN
                      verification operation defined by EMV Co. User applies this message to make PIN pad
                      ask cardholder enter his PIN on PIN pad and then PIN pad will send the PIN block (in
                      clear -text or cipher-text) to IC card. For secure consideration, user will not get any
                      information about cardholder’s PIN, user can only get the PIN verification result from
                      PIN pad. In PIN verification operation, PIN pad will issue “Get Challenge” command to
                      get random number from smart card for PIN encryption.
    NOTE: This message is not available in the PTS + SRED configuration.


    Message element:
                                        Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field        Length                Value and description

                    <STX>              1            <02>
                    I08                3            Message ID
                    [Mode]             1            0: Clear PIN mode
                                                    1: Cipher PIN mode
                    <FS>               1            <1C>
                    [Prompt       &    var          ASCII string. The data in this filed is used to
                    Amount]                         present to cardholder. The length of this filed
                                                    could be 1 ~ 32. Ex. Amount: 12.345
                                                    PIN pad will append messages on line 3 and
                                                    4 to inform user to make a confirmation.
                    <FS>               1            <1C> (Optional, if Mode is 1)
                    [RSA public        1            (Optional, if Mode is 1)
                    Exp]                            1: 3
                                                    2: 2^16 + 1
                    <FS>               1            (Optional, if Mode is 1)
                                                    <1C>
                    [RSA public        Var.         Hex string. RSA modulus. (Optional, if
                    Modulus]                        Mode is 1)
                    <ETX>              1            <03>
                    [LRC]              1            Checksum


===== PDF PAGE 317 =====

    Message flow:
                            HOST               Direction                A10-P

                    Message I08                    →
                                                           <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                          <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                           <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                           Communication with card.
                                                   
                                                           Message I09 or I0F
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)

Message I09               Response of Smart Card Offline PIN Verification (EMV)
    Format:               <STX>I09[Response]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)
    Message length: Fixed 7 bytes.
    Usage:                PIN pad will return the result of PIN verification operation. For EMV rule, it’s
                          recommended that terminal applies next CVM if get response equal to ‘C’, ‘E’, and ‘S’,
                          terminates EMV transaction if get response equal to ‘D’ and ‘F’.
    NOTE: This message is not available in the PTS + SRED configuration.


    Message element:
                                         Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                          Field          Length                Value and description

                    <STX>              1             <02>
                    I09                3             Message ID
                     [Response]            1          ASCII character.
                                                      A: PIN is verified successfully.
                                                      C: Cardholder bypass PIN.
                                                      D: Communication failed (with smart card).
                                                      E: Exceed PIN try limit
                                                      S: No random number from
                                                         smart card.
                                                      F: Other errors
                    <ETX>              1             <03>
                    [LRC]              1             Checksum


    Message flow:
        Refer to message I08.

Message I0F               Error Codes
    Format:               <STX>I0F[error code]<ETX>[LRC] (error frame)


    Message length: Fixed 8 bytes.
    Usage:                This message contains the error code indicating what’s going on with the erroneous
                          situation. The error code list is as follows:
                          "01" - ATR error or NO smart card.
                          "02" - APDU data length is not enough.
                          "03" – ICC/SAM card not powered yet, use I01 first.
                          "04" - The alphanumeric value in the APDU data unit is out of range.
                          "05" - Current smart card reader doesn't have card in.
                          "06" - Smart card interface switch failed.
                          "07" - A10-P Smart card module timed out.
                          "08" - Smart card APDU command failed.
                          ”09” - This parameter is not supported by A10-P.
                          ”0A” - This unit does not have the corresponding peripheral (ICC reader or SAM).
                          ”0B” – Already powered up.


    Message element:
                                            Error frame (A10-P to HOST)
                           Field         Length                Value and description

                    <STX>               1            <02>
                    I0F                 3            Message ID
                    [Error code]        2            Error code
                    <ETX>               1            <03>
                    [LRC]               1            Checksum


    Message flow:
        Refer to I00 to I07.

Message I14               SAM slot Card Deactivate
    Format:               <STX>I14<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:                Upon receiving the card deactivate packet, A10-P sets the active smart card into the
                          deactivate state. If the operation is successful, A10-P will reply the same packet to
                          confirm. Otherwise, message I0F with the error code will be sent.


    Message element:
                                           Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field          Length               Value and description

                    <STX>              1             <02>
                    I14                3             Message ID
                    <ETX>              1             <03>
                    [LRC]              1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                               HOST               Direction                  A10-P

                    Message I14                       →
                                                                <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                               <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                Deactivate smart card.
                                                               Message I14 (success)
                                                                Message I0F (fail)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                   →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 323 =====

Message I15            SAM Select Interface
    Format:            <STX>I15[IF_code]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 7 bytes.
    Usage:             A10-P sets the active interface of the smart card reader. If the operation succeeds,
                       A10-P will reply the same packet to confirm. Otherwise, message I0F with error code is
                       sent. All SAM slot commands will be sent to the card via this active interface.


    Message element:
                                         Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                           Field     Length                Value and description

                      <STX>          1          <02>
                      I15            3          Message ID
                      [IF_code]      1          Interface code.
                                                '1' = SAM 1
                                                '2' = SAM 2
                      <ETX>          1          <03>
                      [LRC]          1          Checksum


    Message flow:
                             HOST               Direction                   A10-P

                    Message I15                     →
                                                              <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                             <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                              <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                              Message I15
                                                    
                                                              Message I0F (fail)
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)                 →
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 324 =====

Message I16               SAM slot C-APDU
    Format:               <STX>I16[C-APDU]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Variable 14 to 530 bytes.
    Usage:                The     C-APDU (application protocol data unit) is the data to be sent to the active smart
                          card. A10-P 's SAM reader will handle with base protocols such as T=0 and T=1,            and
                          the programmer only needs sending out the APDU. In general, A10-P will reply
                          message I17 (response APDU from card) to the host in 1 second. For any error, A10-P
                          will send back message I0F with an error code.


    Message element:
                                             Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                          Field            Length                Value and description

                    <STX>                1             <02>
                    I16                  3             Message ID
                    [C-APDU]             8..524        Smart card command APDU.
                                                       Format: hexadecimal string.
                    <ETX>                1             <03>
                    [LRC]                1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                                 HOST               Direction                   A10-P

                    Message I15                        →         (Select interface and respond)
                    Message I11                                   (Smart card power on and ATR
                                                       →
                                                                  response)
                    Message I16                         →
                                                                  <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                                 <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                                  <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                                  Communication with card.
                                                        
                                                                  Message I17 or I0F
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                    <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)


===== PDF PAGE 325 =====

Message I17               SAM slot R-APDU
    Format:               <STX>I17[R-APDU]<ETX>[LRC] (response frame)


    Message length: Variable 10 to 530 bytes.
    Usage:                After SAM card processes the Command-APDU, it puts the result into the
                          Response-APDU. A10-P packs the Response-APDU into this message packet and
                          send it to the host.


    Message element:
                                         Response frame (A10-P to HOST)
                           Field         Length                Value and description

                    <STX>               1            <02>
                    I17                 3            Message ID
                    [R-APDU]            4.. 524      Smart card response APDU
                                                     Format: hexadecimal string.
                    <ETX>               1            <03>
                    [LRC]               1            Checksum


    Message flow:
        Refer to message I16.


===== PDF PAGE 326 =====

Message Q1               Start MSR Reading
    Format:              <STX>Q1<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 5 bytes.
    Usage:               The A10-P arms the MSR for read and displays the “SWIPE CARD” prompt on its LCD
                         until a card is swiped through the reader slot, or message Z1 (reset state) is received,
                         or the CAN key is pressed.
                         After the magnetic stripe card is swiped, A10-P will collect and decode the card data
                         and do formatting designated by message Q6. The data will be output to the host by
                         message 81 and the “PIN PAD PROCESSING” message is displayed.
    NOTE: For first time use of the A10-P, issue message Q4 to enable the MSR tracks before issuing Q1.


    Message element:
                                          Request frame (HOST to A10-P)
                         Field          Length               Value and description

                    <STX>             1             <02>
                    Q1                2             Message ID
                    <ETX>             1             <03>
                    [LRC]             1             Checksum


    Message flow:
                              HOST               Direction                  A10-P

                    Message Q1                       →
                                                               “SWIPE CARD” displayed on the
                                                               LCD
                                                               <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                              <NAK> (Bad LRC)
                                                               <EOT> (after 3 NAKs)
                                                               (User swipes the magnetic card)
                                                     
                                                               Message 81
                    <ACK> (Good LRC)
                                                     →
                    <NAK> (Bad LRC)

Message Q4               Enable/Disable Magnetic Card Reader
    Format:              <STX>Q4[flag]<ETX>[LRC] (request frame)


    Message length: Fixed 6 bytes.
    Usage:               All   tracks are disabled by default (after each re-power on the A10-P). The host can
                         use this message to enable or disable the magnetic card reader of the A10-P. Only
                         track 2 is enabled if the [flag] field contains ‘0’. All tracks will be enabled if the [flag]

    NOTE: Please refer to message 81 for the returned magnetic card data format.

                                                      ‘1’: disable
                                                      ‘2’: enable all tracks of MSR
                    <ETX>               1             <03>
                    [LRC]               1             Checksum
